# Fragment issues — section 5b, rows 176 to 200

> Rows 176 to 200 of section 5b of [the register](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md), in their own file since 2026-09-23 so that
> they can be read alone. **The section's own rules are on that page, under its heading.** A new
> row goes at the end of the section's last file, whatever its number, and the next run of
> [`tools/split-register.py`](../../tools/split-register.py) moves it to the file its number
> belongs to. [`tools/register-text.py`](../../tools/register-text.py) reads every row back into
> the register for every tool that reads it, so a row here is seen exactly as it was seen there.

| # | Defect | State |
|---|---|---|
| **176** | **`FIND_CLASHES` COUNTED AN ELEMENT IT COULD NOT TEST AS CLEAR.** Found 2026-09-23 checking the plan's row 6 - the same trap was observed elsewhere in another tool's clash check. `brain/fragments/find-clashes/impl/any/fragment.cs`: when `ElementIntersectsElementFilter` threw - a view, a group, an element whose geometry is not generated - the element went into `clear`, and the header said that was true because *"nothing was found to intersect them"*. Nothing was looked for. An empty `against` counted every element clear as well. A clash report calling an untested element clear is the line a coordination meeting acts on without checking. | **FIXED 2026-09-23.** Such an element goes to a new `notChecked`, with Revit's reason in a new `findings`; an empty `against` counts nothing clear; clashing, clear and notChecked add up to what was handed in. The old negative case expected *"counted in clear"* and was corrected. DRAFT, so no proof moved. NEEDS-CHECKING AJ6 **RUN 2026-09-23 on the owner's PC - [NEEDS-CHECKING AJ6](../needs-checking/group-aj.md):** a model group in the batch went to `notChecked`, not `clear`, and 1 clash + 2 clear + 1 not checked made the 4 handed in. An empty `against` never reaches the fragment - the binder refuses `selected` with nothing selected. Proof drafted, not signed. |
| **177** | **`FIND_DEAD_ENDS` AND `FIND_SYSTEM_ISLANDS` DID NOT KNOW THE PLUMBING EQUIPMENT CATEGORY EXISTED.** Found 2026-09-23 checking the plan's row 6, marked *reported* as "new in 2024". In the files: `find-dead-ends` decides "served" by four categories and `find-system-islands` decides "a source" by three, and neither lists Plumbing Equipment. **The plan's year is one release late.** Read off the reference packages with the dump mode of `tools/api-surface`, `BuiltInCategory.OST_PlumbingEquipment` is absent in 2020, 2021 and 2022 and present from the 2023.1 package on. So on a model that uses it, a run ending at a water heater read as a dead end and its network as sourceless. Fire Protection, Food Service Equipment, Medical Equipment and Mechanical Control Devices also arrived after 2020, and whether each counts is **not decided**. | **FIXED 2026-09-23, NOT PROVEN.** Plumbing Equipment is looked up by name at run time (`Enum.TryParse`) and added where the release has it: the spelled-out member does not compile on 2020 to 2022, and a version `#if` cannot stand in (row 5b-181). **Both were PROVEN and are now DRAFT with their proofs kept** - the fingerprint covers `impl/`, and a proof describing code that no longer runs is replaced rather than left standing as PROVEN (row 69). Compiles on all eight releases. NEEDS-CHECKING AJ6 is the re-proof |
| **178** | **`COLOR_BY_PARAMETER` AND `OVERRIDE_GRAPHICS_IN_VIEW` LEFT INSULATION AND LINING IN THE OLD COLOUR, WHILE `HIGHLIGHT_VS_REST` HAS FOLLOWED THE OWNER'S RULE SINCE IT WAS WRITTEN.** The rule - insulation and lining take the colour of the run they wrap, *"when you colour ducts or duct accessories, colour their insulation too"* - is recorded in `highlight-vs-rest`'s card and carried out there (`wrapsFollowed`). The plan's row 7 asks the colour fragments to follow it. `color-by-parameter` and `override-graphics-in-view` overrode the elements handed in and nothing else, so a coloured duct sat inside a jacket of another colour. Found 2026-09-23. | **FIXED 2026-09-23, NOT PROVEN.** Both now pass the run's colour, or its whole look, to its own insulation and lining where the view shows them, and report `wrapsFollowed`. **One way only, run to wrap**, so a look given to the insulation alone - a grayout's grey wrapper - never drags the duct with it. **For whoever builds the grayout's steps (package C5)**: services brought forward through OVERRIDE_GRAPHICS_IN_VIEW now bring their insulation too, and an element override beats a category one, so the grey wrapper has to go on the insulation AFTER the services, as element overrides. `set-category-solid-fill` is not changed - by category the rule has no single answer, and its card says why. **Both were PROVEN and are now DRAFT with their proofs kept.** Compiles on all eight releases. NEEDS-CHECKING AJ7 **RUN 2026-09-23 on the owner's PC - [NEEDS-CHECKING AJ7](../needs-checking/group-aj.md):** each wrap took its run's colour in a plan and a 3D view, read back, `wrapsFollowed` 3; a look on the insulation alone left the ducts red, `wrapsFollowed` 0. With the solid fill the duct cannot be seen inside its jacket in plan - recorded for the owner. Both re-proofs drafted, not signed. |
| **179** | **`DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS` BUILT A LINE IN MODEL COORDINATES FROM `Level.Elevation`.** Found 2026-09-23 checking the plan's row 8, marked *reported*. `brain/fragments/dimension-grids-and-levels/impl/any/fragment.cs:184` put `level.Elevation` into the positions that set the dimension line's ends in model coordinates. Autodesk's API reference - read through search results, because the reference pages themselves are blocked from the container - gives `ProjectElevation` as measured from the project origin whatever the level type's Elevation Base, and `Elevation` as measured from that base. Five other fragments already rest on that reading (`report-level-elevations`, `measure-ceiling-height`, `create-roof` among them). Revit measures a dimension from its references, so the numbers on the string were never affected; where the line sat was off by the base's offset, and two level types on different bases could refuse a real string as *"would measure zero"*. | **FIXED 2026-09-23, NOT PROVEN** - `ProjectElevation`. Was PROVEN, now DRAFT with the proof kept. A model whose two elevations agree cannot tell old from new, so the re-proof needs a level type based on the survey point. NEEDS-CHECKING AJ8 |
| **180** | **TWO FRAGMENTS DESCRIBE `Level.Elevation` AND `Level.ProjectElevation` THE OPPOSITE WAY ROUND FROM FIVE OTHERS - AND ONE OF THEM COMPUTES WITH ITS DESCRIPTION.** Found 2026-09-23 beside row 5b-179. `list-levels/impl/any/fragment.cs` says *"Elevation from the project's INTERNAL ORIGIN"* and *"ProjectElevation from the project's ELEVATION BASE - the number Revit puts on the level head"*; `create-level/impl/any/fragment.cs` says the same and applies `existing.ProjectElevation - existing.Elevation` as a shift before `Level.Create`. `report-level-elevations`, `measure-ceiling-height`, `create-roof`, `check-valve-accessibility`, `create-ceiling`, `create-floor` and `create-wall` rest on the other reading, which is Autodesk's (row 5b-179). **Not checked**: what Revit 2024 returns for each on a survey-based model, and what `Level.Create`'s elevation is measured from - both need a model. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** Neither fragment is in package C4's scope. If `create-level`'s reading is the wrong one, its shift goes the wrong way on exactly the models where it matters |
| **181** | **THE ADD-IN COMPILES FRAGMENTS WITH NO RELEASE SYMBOLS, SO EVERY VERSION `#if` IN A FRAGMENT RUNS ITS `#else` - AND ON 2020 AND 2021 THAT BRANCH CALLS MEMBERS THOSE RELEASES DO NOT HAVE.** Found 2026-09-23 while choosing how to add a category that exists only from the 2023.1 API (row 5b-177). `RevitFragment.Compile` (`revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs`, around lines 816-856) builds `ScriptOptions` with references and imports and nothing else, `CSharpScript.Create` parses with no preprocessor symbols, and `grep '#define' revit/` finds none. `tools/check-fragments-compile.py` compiles the same files WITH the `REVIT<year>` symbols (row 5b-134 is its copy of them), so the gate and the add-in compile different code. Five fragments carry a version `#if`, and **every `#else` calls a member the older releases lack** - read off the reference packages: `arrange-tags` and `arrange-tags-to-view-edges` call `IndependentTag.GetTaggedReferences`, `GetLeaderEnd` and `SetLeaderEnd` (absent in 2020 and 2021); `find-untagged-elements` calls `GetTaggedLocalElementIds` (absent in 2020 and 2021); `audit-view-filters` calls `View.GetIsFilterEnabled` (absent in 2020); `create-view-filter` calls the two-argument `CreateEqualsRule` (absent in 2020, 2021 and 2022). So on those releases the add-in would compile the newer branch and fail, while `check-fragments-compile` reports all five compiling everywhere. **Not checked**: a run on Revit 2020, which is what would show it. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** The add-in is package C3's file and the five fragments are outside C4. The repair is in one place: hand the release's symbols to the script parser, or have the compile gate also compile WITHOUT symbols so it sees what the add-in sees. Until then a fragment must not rely on a version `#if` - row 5b-177's fix looks its category up at run time for exactly this reason |
| **182** | **FIVE PYTHON FILES RESOLVE A WINDOWS SPECIAL FOLDER OUTSIDE THE TWO PATH OWNERS, AND THE RULE THAT FORBIDS IT CANNOT SEE ONE OF THEM.** Found 2026-09-23 while fixing [row 5b-168](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md#). [`tools/check-structure.py`](../../tools/check-structure.py) lets only `HeronPaths.cs` and `heron_bridge_client.py` resolve a special folder, because *two parts once disagreed about where a log lived* - but its pattern matches `environ[...]` and never `environ.get(...)`, and every Python file reads the variable with `.get`. So [`brain/heron_scope.py`](../../brain/heron_scope.py) (knowledge), [`brain/heron_gaps.py`](../../brain/heron_gaps.py) (audit), [`mcp/client/heron_config.py`](../../mcp/client/heron_config.py) (config) and [`tools/heron-backup.py`](../../tools/heron-backup.py) (the whole data folder) each build a Heron folder from `%APPDATA%`, and [`mcp/server/heron_workflow.py`](../../mcp/server/heron_workflow.py) builds a DERIVED checkpoint folder from `%LOCALAPPDATA%` with a fallback of its own, `$TMPDIR` or `/tmp`, that no path owner knows about. The gate passes. **WHAT IS NOT WRONG, TRACED:** on Windows each reads the variable HeronPaths would, and off Windows the four `%APPDATA%` readers answer *nowhere* rather than a relative path - so nothing is known to disagree today. **WHAT WAS NOT CHECKED:** where the checkpoint folder lands against HeronPaths.Derived off Windows, which is exactly row 5b-168's shape. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** Widening the pattern turns the gate red on five files at once, and whether each becomes a path owner or asks one is a design choice. |
| **183** | **`docs/PROJECT-MAP.md` SAYS CI DECIDES ON TEN GATES; IT HAS BEEN ELEVEN SINCE #311.** Found 2026-09-23 while correcting that page's `.claude/` row for [row 5b-169](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md#). Its row for *what to run before pushing* says *"the **ten** CI's job of that name actually decides on"*. [#311](https://github.com/Ajmalpshaik/Heron-AI/pull/311) added `check-decision-titles` and named five pages it changed to *eleven*; this was not one of them, and nothing derives the number, so no gate noticed. It is the third typed gate count to go stale on the day its gate count moved. | **FIXED 2026-09-23, on the owner's instruction** (*"fix row 5b-183 next"*), the way this row asked: the sentence names no number now. [`docs/PROJECT-MAP.md`](../PROJECT-MAP.md) says the skill names the four gates run before pushing *"and every gate CI's job of that name actually decides on, which `.github/workflows/gates.yml` lists"*, with a link to that file, so the next gate added cannot make the sentence wrong. The same stale count was searched for elsewhere: the other *ten gates* are dated records - a session note in `HANDOVER.md` and two results in the NEEDS-CHECKING register - each true on its day and left as written. `AGENTS.md` and the heron-ship skill type *eleven*, which is right today. |
| **184** | **CHECK-DOCS COMPARES A DECISION REFERENCE WITH THE LOG ONLY WHEN ITS NUMBER HAS TWO DIGITS, AND THE LOG NOW RUNS TO D-103.** Found 2026-09-23 while restoring D-101 to D-103 ([row 5b-171](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md#)). [`tools/check-docs.py`](../../tools/check-docs.py) takes the defined decisions from every `## D-` heading, whatever the length of the number, but finds the references with a pattern of exactly two digits between word boundaries - so a reference to D-100 or above never reaches *REFERENCED BUT NOT DEFINED*, and a mistyped three-digit reference anywhere in the repository passes. Derive what it is not comparing: `git grep -o -h -E '[^0-9A-Za-z-]D-[0-9]{3}([^0-9]\|$)' -- '*.md' '*.py' \| grep -o -E 'D-[0-9]+' \| sort -u`. | **FIXED 2026-09-23, on the owner's instruction** (*"fix row 5b-173 next and fix all like that"* - this row was announced as 5b-173 and then as 5b-182, and #309 and #316 took those numbers first). [`tools/check-docs.py`](../../tools/check-docs.py) reads a reference with any number of digits, the way it reads a definition, and section 9 of [`tests/test_docs_guard.py`](../../tests/test_docs_guard.py) plants an undefined three-digit reference beside a defined D-98: red before the fix, green after. **What it reported on its first run was read before it was trusted:** one reference, and it was this row's own example of a mistyped number, written as a literal decision number - it now says *a mistyped three-digit reference*, and the report is empty. The doors marked *D-101* in the dated session note of 2026-09-02 are not reported, because D-101 is defined. **Fix all like that:** the same blindness - a pattern of fixed width over a number that grows - was searched for across the repository. One more was found and fixed: [`tests/test_library_total.py`](../../tests/test_library_total.py) read whole-library totals of exactly three digits, so a total typed after the library passed 999 would have gone by unread; it now reads three digits or more, with self-checks that fail on the old pattern and pass on the new. Left as they are, each for its reason: Revit release years, the fragment and agent id formats (a wrong width fails them loudly), and the numbered steps of one fixed pipeline. |
| **185** | **D-48 IS CITED AS THE RULE THAT `brain` MAY NOT IMPORT `mcp`, AND D-48 SAYS NOTHING ABOUT LAYERS.** Found 2026-09-23 while reading every citation of D-47, D-48 and D-49 for [row 5b-171](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md#). D-48 is *"One broken part costs one part, never the whole library"*, and the first D-48, now D-102, was about several fragments feeding one; neither states a layering rule. Yet `brain/` modules such as `heron_hr.py`, `heron_scaffold.py` and `heron_fix.py`, agent contracts under `brain/agents/`, their suites and `docs/PROPOSALS.md` give *"brain may not read mcp (D-48)"* or *"D-48's layering"* as the authority. The rule itself is real - the `ALLOWED` table in [`tools/check-structure.py`](../../tools/check-structure.py), drawn from [docs/02 §7](../02-architecture-overview.md) - so nothing is wrongly allowed; a reader who follows the citation finds a different decision. `check-structure.py` may be where it started: it says the imports it first found were gates in `tools/` reading `brain/`, *"which is what D-48 asks those gates to do"* - true of D-48, and a different claim from *brain may not import mcp*. Derive the citations: `git grep -n -E 'D-48' -- brain tools tests docs \| grep -i -E 'mcp\|layer'`. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired**, by the rule at the head of this section. The repair starts with a decision to cite - one that already owns the layering, or a new one written for it - and that choice is the owner's; the citations follow it. |
| **186** | **`tests/test_heron_session.py` CRASHES ON THE OWNER'S PC AT LINE 512: ITS CHECK THAT THE HOOKS' DIARY IS OUTSIDE THE REPOSITORY CALLS `os.path.commonpath` ACROSS TWO DRIVES, AND 19 OF ITS 68 CHECKS NEVER REPORT.** Found 2026-09-23 on the owner's PC, running the suites that read the register after recording [Group AI](../needs-checking/group-ai.md). [`tests/test_heron_session.py:512`](../../tests/test_heron_session.py) checks `os.path.commonpath([os.path.realpath(logs), os.path.realpath(ROOT)]) != os.path.realpath(ROOT)`. `logs` is under the suite's `tempfile.mkdtemp()` home, which on that PC is `C:\Users\…\AppData\Local\Temp`, and `ROOT` is the checkout on `D:`. Given two drives, `commonpath` does not answer *different* - it raises `ValueError: Paths don't have the same drive`. So the suite ends in a traceback and exits 1 after 49 checks said `ok`, and `check-gaps.py` there prints it as `FAIL`. **The claim it was checking is TRUE**: a folder on another drive is certainly outside the repository. **The two hooks it tests are not affected** - `top_folder()` in `heron_guard.py` and `_inside()` in `hook_log.py` both catch that `ValueError`, the second saying *"Different drives on Windows: certainly not inside."* - and they hold the only other `commonpath` calls in tracked Python. **MEASURED, not inferred:** the same suite with `TMPDIR`, `TEMP` and `TMP` pointed at `D:` exits 0 with all 68 `ok`, so this line is the only thing in it that fails there. Linux has one root and CI can never raise it. It is the class of [row 5b-17](#) and [row 5b-152](#), whose audit of `os.path.relpath` in `tools/`, `brain/` and `mcp/` came a day before this suite arrived in #308, did not reach `tests/`, and never names `commonpath`. **Not checked:** `os.path.relpath` anywhere in `tests/`. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired**, by the rule at the head of this section, and because the owner asked for this run's findings to be recorded, not fixed. The repair is the suite's own check reading that `ValueError` as *outside*, as both hooks already do. |
| **187** | **`CONNECT_AIR_TERMINALS` ON REVIT 2024 LIFTED EVERY TERMINAL ONTO ITS DUCT'S UNDERSIDE, MADE NO TAP, AND JOINED A SUPPLY DIFFUSER TO A RETURN DUCT - THE CARD SAYS REVIT DECLINES THAT, AND THE HEADER SAYS IT CUTS A TAP.** Found 2026-09-23 on the owner's PC, running [NEEDS-CHECKING AJ1](../needs-checking/group-aj.md) on the test project `Heron-C3-test`, Revit 2024 (session 3544), the add-in built from `main` at `173a4524`. Three `M_Supply Diffuser: 600 x 600 Face 300 x 300 Connection` under 400 x 300 ducts whose underside was at 2850 mm: one at 2300 under the supply duct, one at 2748 with its neck touching the supply duct, one at 2300 under the return duct. `connect-air-terminals` with apply and a reach (`maxDistanceMm`) of 1000 returned `connected` 3, `refused` 0, and `moved` *925837: moved 550.0 mm (z +550.0), 925838: moved 101.6 mm (z +101.6), 925839: moved 550.0 mm (z +550.0)*, with *"925839 tapped into 925835"* - the return duct. The owner's spot elevations read 2300, 2748, 2300 before and 2850, 2850, 2850 after, each diffuser drawn inside its duct, and the model held no duct fitting at all afterwards. So `MechanicalUtils.ConnectAirTerminalOnDuct` put each terminal's insertion point - its face - on the duct's underside and joined it there, and did not look at the system classification. The card's purpose says *"a supply diffuser will not tap a return duct"* and the header *"ConnectAirTerminalOnDuct creates the tap fitting on the duct, positions it under the terminal's connector"*: on this release neither is what happened. **What worked, measured:** `moved` reported every lift exactly as the owner measured it by hand, and nothing was listed at 0.0 mm. **Not checked:** Revit 2020, where the other tool saw 625 mm; a round duct; another diffuser family; a routing preference other than `Radius Elbows / Taps`. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired**, as the owner asked for this run. The tolerance question - how far a terminal may move before Heron calls it a problem - was put to him once with these numbers and is his; so is whether ceiling diffusers should be connected this way at all. The proof he accepted on 2026-09-23 records exactly this behaviour. |
| **188** | **`SET_CATEGORY_SOLID_FILL` ANSWERED `overridden 6` WHILE REVIT KEPT NO FILL ON ANY OF THE SIX MEP CATEGORIES IT WAS HANDED.** Found 2026-09-23 on the owner's PC, running [NEEDS-CHECKING AJ7](../needs-checking/group-aj.md) on `Heron-C3-test`, Revit 2024. In the floor plan `1 - Mech`, which has no view template, `report-category-overrides` read *"0 of 11 category(ies) present in '1 - Mech' carry an override"*; `set-category-solid-fill` on Ducts, Pipes, Air Terminals, Sprinklers, Cable Trays and Conduits with `surface-colour=0,120,255`, kept, answered `overridden 6`, `refused 0`, `notCuttable 6`, `solidPattern` *Solid fill*; and the same reader, seconds later, still found 0 of 11 - though Ducts and Air Terminals both have elements in that view. Sent again through `revit_change` on Air Terminals alone: `overridden 1`, and nothing but the line colour already there. **The reader works:** the owner then set a blue line on Ducts and Air Terminals by hand in Visibility/Graphics, and it read *"Ducts: line RGB(0,0,255)"* and *"Air Terminals: line RGB(0,0,255)"* at once. The fragment counts every `SetCategoryOverrides` call that did not throw ([`impl/any/fragment.cs`](../../brain/fragments/set-category-solid-fill/impl/any/fragment.cs)) and reads nothing back, so a fill Revit did not keep is counted the same as one it did. **Not checked:** why - by the reader's own header its fill did land on Walls in Project1 on 2026-09-22, and Walls are cuttable where these six are not; Revit 2020. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** The fragment is PROVEN on another model, so a code change makes its proof stale and is another session's. The repair is to read back what landed and count that. |
| **189** | **`heron_resolve` STILL TAKES A CAPABILITY'S STATE FROM THE KNOWLEDGE STORE: IT CALLED FOUR FRAGMENTS `PROVEN` THAT HAD BEEN `DRAFT` ON DISK SINCE #309, WHILE ITS OWN FOOTER COUNTED THE DISK.** Found 2026-09-23 on the owner's PC, in the session that ran NEEDS-CHECKING Group AJ, with the MCP server started at 19:11 on `main` at `173a4524`. `heron_resolve` answered *"best state PROVEN ... FRG-VIEW-001 PROVEN"* for `OVERRIDE_GRAPHICS_IN_VIEW` and `PROVEN` for `COLOR_BY_PARAMETER`, `FIND_DEAD_ENDS` and `DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS` - all four `heron-status: DRAFT` in their `fragment.yaml` since C4 sent them back - while the same answers ended *"323 of 396 capabilities are PROVEN"*, the count on disk. `CONNECT_AIR_TERMINALS`, DRAFT before C4 and after, read DRAFT. The state comes from the store: `resolve()` in [`mcp/server/heron_brain.py`](../../mcp/server/heron_brain.py) reads `store.fragments()`. [Row 127 of section 5](section-5-rows-126-150.md) fixed this for `ask()` and `heron_retrieve.find()`; `resolve()` is a third route that fix did not reach, and row 131 of section 5 is why the store can lag a worktree. A chat reading *"best state PROVEN"* runs a fragment whose proof no longer describes its code. **Not checked:** which worktree last indexed the store, and whether `heron_capabilities` shares the route. | **FIXED 2026-09-29.** `heron_capability.resolve()` - the route `heron_resolve` and `heron_capabilities` both read - now caps each provider's status at its fragment's own file through `heron_search.disk_status`, row 127's shallow disk read. **One way only, as `may_run_unasked`**: the file can LOWER what the index says and never raise it, and a fragment with no file keeps the index's word. `tests/test_capability.py` section 7 stages the defect - a real DRAFT fragment indexed as PROVEN - and was seen to FAIL before the change (`-> PROVEN`) and pass after (`-> DRAFT`). Golden Rule 11. |
| **190** | **`heron_resolve` TOLD EVERY PLAIN `Element` NEED TO TYPE AN ELEMENT TYPE BY NAME, AND THE ADD-IN REFUSES A TYPED NAME FOR EVERY ONE OF THEM.** Found 2026-09-23 on the owner's PC, against `Project1` on Revit 2024, through the heron MCP tools. `heron_resolve FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_TYPE` listed, under *YOU SUPPLY THESE*, `exemplar  Element  an element TYPE by name - "Basic Wall: Generic - 200mm"`. Followed exactly - `revit_read FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_TYPE` with `exemplar=M_Supply Diffuser: 600 x 600 Face 300 x 300 Connection` - it was refused by `OneElement` in [`RevitFragment.cs`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs): *"'exemplar' asks for ONE PARTICULAR ELEMENT in the model, and a typed name cannot say which one ... SELECT IT IN REVIT and pass "selected""*, and the fragment's own proof ran with `exemplar=selected`. **The cause:** a plain `Element` is a type to build with or one particular element, and only the need's NAME says which - `IsTypeNeedName`, a name ending in `Type`. [`how_to_type`](../../brain/heron_fragment.py) took the type alone, and `_contract_lines` in [`heron_mcp_server.py`](../../mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py) asked it with the type alone, so its last rule, `\bElement\b`, answered TYPE for every one - under a comment saying *"Only a `...Type` need reaches this hint now - one meaning a particular element is refused above and never emitted"*. That was true of [`tools/generate-jobs.py`](../../tools/generate-jobs.py), whose `receivable` blocks such a need, and false of `heron_resolve` - and of [`tools/prove-skill.py`](../../tools/prove-skill.py), which emits them and had grown its own `correct_the_hint` to cover the same hint. **Measured across the library** by running the server's own contract block for every capability: of 765 caller-supplied values, the TYPE hint sat on exactly the 16 plain `Element` needs, in 15 fragments, and none of the 16 is named like a type - so every one was told the one thing the add-in refuses. **Not checked:** a live `heron_resolve` - the MCP server reads the brain when it starts, so it shows the new hint only once this is merged and the Claude app restarted. | **FIXED 2026-09-23.** `how_to_type(declared, need_name)` answers a plain `Element` from the need's name by the add-in's rule, now `is_type_need_name` in `heron_fragment.py` (moved from `generate-jobs.py`, which re-exports it): a type-named need is told a type name, and every other - and a call with no name, as in the add-in - is told *ONE PARTICULAR element - SELECT IT IN REVIT and type `selected`*. The `\bElement\b` rule and its comment are gone, so nothing merely containing the word inherits a type hint. `heron_resolve` and `generate-jobs.py` pass the name, and the other 749 hints are byte-identical. Clause 8 of [`tests/test_how_to_type.py`](../../tests/test_how_to_type.py) reads `IsTypeNeedName`, `IsSelectionWord` and `OneElement`'s refusal out of the C# and holds every plain `Element` need in the library to them - through `how_to_type`, through `heron_resolve`'s own contract block run from the server's source, and through a made-up fragment holding one need of each kind in both printers. On the old code it fails 36 checks cleanly, and each printer's check goes red alone when that printer drops the name. Nothing in the add-in or in any fragment changed. `measure-distance` and `measure-available-fall` take two such elements, and one selection fills both with the same one: [section 6](section-6.md) records that already, and both fragments say so in their findings rather than answering - a hint cannot change it. |
| **191** | **THE `ElementId` HINTS ARE NAME-BLIND TOO: FOUR CALLER-SUPPLIED ID NEEDS ARE TOLD TO TYPE A NAME, AND `OneIdNamed` HAS NO RULE FOR ANY OF THEIR NAMES.** Found 2026-09-23 checking every plain `Element` need for [row 5b-190](#). An id is resolved by the need's NAME - `OneIdNamed` in [`RevitFragment.cs`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs) finds a level for `levelId` and a sheet for `sheetId` - and a name it has no rule for is refused *"rather than searching every element ... and binding whatever happened to match"*. Only a blank or `none`, meaning no element at all, gets past that for any need. [`how_to_type`](../../brain/heron_fragment.py) answers every `ElementId` by its type alone: *"the NAME of the thing, never its number"*, and for a list *"NAMES, never numbers, comma separated - or the word `selected`"*. Four request-sourced needs have no rule, read with `id_need_names()` in [`tools/generate-jobs.py`](../../tools/generate-jobs.py): `create-view-filter`'s `parameterId` (`ElementId`), `filter-elements-by-id`'s `elementIds` and `copy-from-link`'s `linkedElementIds` (`IList<ElementId>`), and `find-clashes`'s `against` (`ICollection<ElementId>`). So `heron_resolve` tells `parameterId` to type a name the add-in refuses whatever the name, and tells the three lists to type names that are refused, beside `selected`, which does bind. `generate-jobs.py` blocks all four already - `receivable` asks `id_need_names` - so only the hint is wrong. **Not checked:** whether `selected` means anything for `linkedElementIds`, whose ids belong to a linked model while the selection is the host's, or for `against`, the second set of a clash check. | **FIXED 2026-09-23.** `id_need_names`, the reader of `OneIdNamed`'s table, moved into [`heron_fragment.py`](../../brain/heron_fragment.py) beside `is_type_need_name`, as row 5b-190 moved that one, and [`generate-jobs.py`](../../tools/generate-jobs.py) re-exports it - still one reader, still reading the C#. `how_to_type` asks it with the need's name. A name with a rule keeps its hint word for word. A name without one is told *CANNOT BE TYPED BY NAME*, and what binds anyway, which the add-in settles before the table is asked: for one id *"it refuses any name, a number or `selected`. Only a blank or `none` binds, and it means NO element"* - a single id goes straight to `OneIdNamed`, which has no selection branch - and for a list *"The word `selected` binds everything selected in Revit, in the model being read, and a blank binds an empty list"*. A list is not offered `none`: `Parts` makes it one invalid id, not none. A call with no name gets the no-rule answer, as the add-in would give it, and an add-in that cannot be read makes the hint say *NOT KNOWN* rather than guess or end the caller's `heron_resolve`. **Measured by running the server's own contract block for every capability:** of 770 hints, the 4 above changed and the other 766 are byte-identical. **What `selected` means for the two lists this row left unchecked**, read from the code and from Autodesk's own reference rather than run: for `against` it MEANS SOMETHING - host ids, which `find-clashes` searches in the host - but run alone, its `elements` is filled from the same selection, so the two sets are one: each element is tested against the others, its own hit removed, and a clashing pair is counted from both sides. It is a second set only when `elements` comes from the fragment before it. For `linkedElementIds` it does NOT - it binds the host's ids and `CopyElements` looks them up in the link's document - recorded as [row 5b-202](#). Clause 9 of [`tests/test_how_to_type.py`](../../tests/test_how_to_type.py) reads the table, the blank and `none` branch, the single id's path and the list's `selected` out of `RevitFragment.cs` and holds every caller-supplied id need in the library to them - it finds them itself, 34 on the day - through `how_to_type`, through `heron_resolve`'s contract block run from the server's source, and through `generate-jobs`' printer on a made-up fragment holding each kind. On the old code it fails 16 checks cleanly, with no traceback; with the server's call dropping the name it fails 31, and with `generate-jobs`' dropping it, 3. Clause 4 asked for the id hint with no name, which is this row's premise; it asks with `levelId` now, and its claim stands. Nothing in the add-in or in any fragment changed. **Not checked:** a live `heron_resolve`, which shows the new hint only once this is merged and the Claude app restarted. |

| **196** | **`tools/change-evidence.py` DECODES WHAT ITS CHILDREN PRINT IN THE WINDOWS CODE PAGE, AND ONE OF ITS READER THREADS DIED ON THE OWNER'S PC.** Found 2026-09-23 while taking the before record for row 5b-195: the capture printed `UnicodeDecodeError: 'charmap' codec can't decode byte 0x8f in position 795` from `subprocess._readerthread` (in `cp1252.py`) and carried on. `run()`, which runs the gates, and the loop that runs the suites both call `subprocess.run(..., capture_output=True, text=True)` with no `encoding=`, so on Windows a child's UTF-8 output is decoded as cp1252 - the reading-side trap that `.claude/skills/heron-ship` section 2 describes for suites, here in the tool that measures them. **What it costs:** the suite loop keeps only the exit code, so that record's verdicts stand; `run()` keeps a gate's last line as its note, and that note comes back empty; and a child that went on printing past a full pipe after the bad byte would stall until its timeout. **Not checked:** which child it was. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired**, by the rule at the head of this section - it was found doing something else. The repair is `encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"` on those two calls, which the suites that read their children already carry. |

| **200** | **THE RE-RANKER'S ANNOUNCEMENT - THE SENTENCE R-77 SAYS A PERSON READS BEFORE AGREEING TO A DOWNLOAD - SAYS 500 MB TO 2 GB, AND ON LINUX PIP FETCHED 3,099 MB. | **203** | **TWENTY FRAGMENTS FIND A PARAMETER BY NAME WITH `LookupParameter`, WHICH AUTODESK DOCUMENTS AS PICKING AT RANDOM WHEN TWO PARAMETERS ON ONE ELEMENT SHARE THAT NAME - AND TEN OF THEM THEN CHANGE THE MODEL WITH WHAT IT PICKED.** Found 2026-09-24 studying a downloaded set of working notes for another product's Revit connector ([39](../39-three-downloaded-skill-sets.md) §3, lesson 6). Those notes record a curtain wall type showing *Interior Type* twice - under Vertical Mullions and under Horizontal Mullions - and a set by that name only ever reaching one of the two. **The rule is Heron's already, twice, and these fragments do not follow it.** [D-54](../DECISIONS.md) §3: a name that matches twice is refused, never chosen from. [`RevitParameters.cs`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitParameters.cs) calls taking the first match *"a coin toss that reads like a fact"*, marks such a row `ambiguous`, and tells the modeller that asking by that name *"would hit whichever Revit returned first"* - which is what these twenty do. **Autodesk's reference says it more strongly**, read off `RevitAPI.xml` in the 2020 and 2024 SDK reference packages on the owner's PC: for `Element.LookupParameter`, when several match, *"the first one encountered will be returned. This match is determined at random and may change in the future"*; for `GetParameters(String)`, a second match happens *"because shared parameters or project parameters can be bound to an element category even if there is a built-in parameter with the same name already"*. So a curtain wall is only the everyday case; a consultant's shared parameter named like a built-in one is the other. **The twenty**, from `grep -rl LookupParameter brain/fragments --include=*.cs`, sorted by each one's own `risk:`. Every one looks up a name that arrives from outside it - typed in the request, read from a CSV header or a standards list - except `check-sleeve-size`, which tries its own list of size names; and `grep -l "GetParameters("` finds a second-match check in none of them. **MODIFY, ten:** `write-element-parameters`, `copy-parameter-value`, `edit-parameter-text`, `edit-text-values`, `import-parameter-values`, `remove-parameter-value`, `renumber-sequential` and `assign-location-data` write a value into what was picked; `create-view-filters-by-value` builds its filter rule on the picked parameter's id; `color-by-parameter` (DRAFT) colours by it. **PUBLISH, one:** `export-parameters-to-csv`. **READ, nine:** `read-element-parameters`, `select-by-parameter-value`, `select-by-numeric-parameter`, `find-duplicate-values`, `compare-elements`, `compare-models`, `check-family-standards`, `check-model-standards` (DRAFT) and `check-sleeve-size`. **WHY NO PROOF COULD SEE IT:** `write-element-parameters` reads back to catch a snapped size, and it looks the name up the same way to do it - so it reads back the parameter it wrote and counts it `written`. A correct write, possibly to the wrong parameter, and every proof so far used a name that occurs once. **ONE CURTAIN WALL PAIR IS REFUSED ALREADY, BY ACCIDENT:** a mullion setting stores an element reference, which `write-element-parameters` refuses by storage type. **Not checked here:** which of a pair Revit returns on a real curtain wall, and whether the grid's *Spacing* and *Layout* repeat their names the way the mullion pair does. Both need a Revit, and [Group AP](../needs-checking/group-ap.md) arranges them. | **OPEN - for the ten that only read. The ten that change the model were repaired in the change that found them, on 2026-09-24 - repaired, not proven.** The repair is the refusal D-54 already states: each of the ten asks `GetParameters` first, and an element on which the name returns more than one parameter - on the instance, or on the type where the fragment falls through to it - is reported and nothing on it is touched. Where the fragment answers in lists it is `ambiguous`, beside `absent` and `readOnly`; where it answers in counted sentences it is a count of its own, named apart because the fix is different. **It is not the section's rule being broken:** the owner's standing instruction is that anything which can change his model without his asking is fixed at once, and a value written to a parameter nobody chose is that. **The cost, stated:** nine of the ten were PROVEN and the refusal is inside `impl/`, so their fingerprints moved and all nine went back to DRAFT with their old proofs kept as a record ([row 5b-69](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md#)'s seal). Their re-proof, and the negative case each one owes, is [Group AP](../needs-checking/group-ap.md) AP8; `color-by-parameter` was DRAFT already. **What is still open is the other ten:** the PUBLISH one and the nine READs still take whichever parameter Revit returns. None of them changes the model - their cost is a confidently wrong answer, which is why they were left for a change of their own rather than widening this one. |
| **205** | **`heron_generate.author` WRITES `heron-agent: HERON-FRG-CRE-007` INTO EVERY FRAGMENT IT MAKES, WHERE THE METADATA STANDARD AND EVERY OTHER FRAGMENT NAME THE AGENT WHOSE JOB THE FRAGMENT DOES.** Found 2026-09-24 when the `heron-fragment-author` agent made `slope-roof-edges` for [39](../39-three-downloaded-skill-sets.md): its card came out claiming the Fragment Creation Agent. And the library agrees with 29: `grep -h '^heron-agent:' brain/fragments/*/fragment.yaml \| sort \| uniq -c` finds HERON-FRG-CRE-007 on none of the other 407 - each names the Revit agent whose job it does, `HERON-REVIT-ELE-010` for `create-roof` and `create-floor`. So `slope-roof-edges` was set to `HERON-REVIT-ELE-010` by hand, like its neighbour, and the next fragment the generator makes will need the same hand. **The cost today is the field's truth and nothing else:** `brain/heron_fragment.py` only checks that the id is in the registry, and a grep of `tools/` and `brain/` found nothing that lists an agent's fragments by it. **Not checked:** whether the family fragments of 2026-09-24 went through the generator and were corrected the same way - they carry `HERON-REVIT-FAM-012`. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired**, by the rule at the head of this section: the generator and its test belong to their own change, and which meaning the field carries on a fragment is a question about [29](../29-metadata-standard.md), not a typo. The likely repair takes the agent from the owner of the card's `domain:`, and keeps the author in a field of its own if it is worth keeping. |
| **211** | **HERON HAD NO ROUTE THAT PUT A DOOR INTO A WALL: ON 2026-09-22 A DOOR WENT IN FREE-STANDING THROUGH `PLACE_FAMILY_INSTANCES`, WHICH REPORTED SUCCESS, BECAUSE `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` COULD NOT BE GIVEN A WALL.** Found 2026-09-22 in Project4 (Revit 2024, session 37184) and written up as item 1 of the section [*A drawing set and a house plan*](a-drawing-set-and-a-house-plan-2026-09-22.md), which gave it no row. `PLACE_FAMILY_INSTANCES` (FRG-ELE-004, PROVEN) was handed M_Single-Flush `0864 x 2134mm` and a point on a wall's centreline and came back `failed 0`, `placed 1 item(s) [352528]`, and the door cut nothing - the wall's `Area` read full. `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` version 1 (FRG-ELE-065, DRAFT) took its host as one `Element`: `OneElement` in [`RevitFragment.cs`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs) refuses a typed name for one particular element, and until [row 5b-190](#) was fixed on 2026-09-23 `heron_resolve` told the caller to type an element TYPE. Only `selected` could reach it, one wall for every point, and it was never run. **Version 2, written 2026-09-24, finds the wall at each point** ([`impl/any/fragment.cs`](../../brain/fragments/place-hosted-family/impl/any/fragment.cs)): the straight walls on the level whose location line passes within half their width plus 1 mm of the point, strictly between the line's ends. No wall puts the point in `noWall`, two within 1 mm of each other put it in `twoWalls`, and a door whose `Host` does not read back as the wall found is deleted inside the same undo and counted in `failed`. **Measured in a chat, KEPT, on 2026-09-24** in Project1 (Revit 2024) with M_Single-Flush `0915 x 2134mm`: 14 doors placed, and the walls' Volume went from 299.36 to 293.893 m³ - 14 x 0.3905 m³, and 0.3905 m³ is 0.915 x 2.134 x the 0.2 m wall - so every door cut its wall. `heron_lookup` resolves *"put a door in this wall"* to `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` by identity, measured the same day against a store built from the change's tree. **Not checked:** any negative case; windows, door families other than M_Single-Flush, curved walls, which version 2 skips by design, and the hinge it reads off a family's plan swing arc - NEEDS-CHECKING AO7 to AO10. | **OPEN - the route is repaired in DRAFT, and the free-standing placement is not.** Version 2 of `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` is the route, not yet proven: its D-30 proof is NEEDS-CHECKING AO6. **What version 2 does not touch:** `PLACE_FAMILY_INSTANCES` still places a wall-hosted family free-standing and counts it - its own TRAP note records the door - and teaching it to refuse one changes its `impl/`, which stales its proof ([row 5b-69](section-5b-rows-051-075.md)), so it waits for its next re-proof. |
| **212** | **A FACING FLIP DOES NOT MOVE A DOOR'S FROM AND TO ROOM - SO `REPORT_DOOR_ROOM_LINKS`, WHICH TAKES "TO ROOM" TO BE "THE ROOM THE DOOR FACES", IS RIGHT ONLY UNTIL SOMEBODY FLIPS THE DOOR.** Measured 2026-09-24 in Project1 (Revit 2024) on M_Single-Flush `0915 x 2134mm`, through `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` version 2 ([row 5b-211](#)). A read probe, deleted afterwards, found each door's plan swing arc on its `FacingOrientation` side. `flipFacing()` moved the swing and **not** the rooms: four doors flipped to swing toward their offices still read To Room Corridor until `FlipFromToRoom()` was called. A re-run over those four came back alreadyThere 4, placed 0, toRoomSwapped 4, and all 14 then swing into their office with To Room Office NN and From Room Corridor 15. **So the facing and the To Room are two settings**: Revit makes them agree when a door is placed, and flipping one leaves the other where it was. [`report-door-room-links`](../../brain/fragments/report-door-room-links/impl/any/fragment.cs) (FRG-GEO-018, DRAFT, READ) is built on the opposite. Lines 7-13 say *"FromRoom AND ToRoom ARE DECIDED BY THE DOOR'S FACING, NOT BY THE ROOMS. Flip a door and the two swap"*, line 129 says *"ToRoom is the room the door FACES"*, and its `fragment.yaml` purpose says the same. It compares the To Room with the room a probe finds ahead along the facing, so a door flipped after it was placed reads as a disagreement, and the reply calls it *"very likely flipped; the geometry is the one to believe"* - of a door whose To Room may be exactly what the modeller meant, such as an office door swinging out into a corridor and still numbered by its office. **Not checked:** a door flipped by hand with Revit's flip arrows rather than through the API - believed to be the same operation, never read; any family but M_Single-Flush; and `report-door-room-links` itself, which has never run. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired**, and the fragment left as it is. It is DRAFT, so a repair stales no proof, and the repair is the owner's to choose: report the swing side and the To Room as two facts, and a disagreement as a door whose swing and whose numbering point different ways, never as a flip to undo. `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` version 2 already sets the two separately. |
| **213** | **`heron_lookup` SENDS *"which rooms are not tagged"* AND *"are all the rooms tagged"* TO `TAG_ROOMS`, A WRITE - AND NO READ IN HERON CAN SAY WHICH ROOMS CARRY A ROOM TAG.** Measured 2026-09-24 on the owner's PC by calling `heron_lookup`'s own function - `lookup` in [`mcp/server/heron_brain.py`](../../mcp/server/heron_brain.py) - for Revit 2024, against two private knowledge stores, one built from `main` at `4154d015` and one from this change's tree; nearness by the trained model, no re-ranker. Three more phrasings: *"is every room tagged"* moved from `FIND_UNTAGGED_ELEMENTS` to `TAG_ROOMS`, *"check the room tags"* from `CENTER_ROOM_TAGS` to `TAG_ROOMS`, and *"which rooms have no tag"* reaches `CENTER_ROOM_TAGS`, a write, on both trees. **[`tools/check-routing.py`](../../tools/check-routing.py) cannot see any of it**: none of these sentences is declared or claimed in a routing table, and it asks only those back. Run on both trees, it reports *"NO QUESTION IS ANSWERED BY SOMETHING THAT WRITES"*, and `TAG_ROOMS` adds nothing to the routing-table claims it cannot reach. What it does show is `TAG_ROOMS` first by words for two sentences other fragments declare - *"which elements are in this room"* (`FILTER_ELEMENTS_IN_ROOM`, READ) and *"put the room tag in the room"* (`CENTER_ROOM_TAGS`) - both still served by identity; and [`tools/check-intrusion.py`](../../tools/check-intrusion.py) puts `TAG_ROOMS` in 51 of the 2300 shortlists it does not own, tied ninth in the library - measured again after `main`'s #332 and #333 were merged in, where it had been 53 of 2290. **The read it used to reach cannot answer either.** [`find-untagged-elements`](../../brain/fragments/find-untagged-elements/impl/any/fragment.cs) (FRG-VIEW-009, PROVEN) collects the view's tags with `OfClass(typeof(IndependentTag))` at line 41, and Revit's room tag is another class, so a room that carries one reads as untagged. [`RevitAnnotation.cs`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitAnnotation.cs) line 116 counts the same class, so `revit_annotation`'s tag count never includes a room tag. `FIND_OVERLAPPING_TAGS` reads room tags only to find overlaps, and `SELECT_BY_CATEGORY_NAME` with `categoryName=Room Tags` selects the tags themselves - it found the 15 `TAG_ROOMS` placed on 2026-09-24 - without saying which room each is on. **Not checked:** any other phrasing. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired**, by the rule at the head of this section. The repair is the owner's to choose: a read that answers the question - `FIND_UNTAGGED_ELEMENTS` taught room tags, which stales its proof, or a new read - declaring these sentences, so identity answers them before any ranking runs. `TAG_ROOMS`' own card records the same finding and was deliberately not reworded to lose its words: that would make a write harder to find in order to protect a measurement, the response `check-routing`'s docstring rules out. |

So `927790, 927821` is refused, by design, and the comment at line 3204 names `linkedElementIds` as refused the same way. And the three fragments that set Revit's selection - `set-selection` (FRG-SEL-001), `update-saved-set` (FRG-SEL-023) and `zoom-to-elements` (FRG-SEL-030), all PROVEN - select the `elements` an earlier step handed in, never ids typed in the request. So every write arrangement with a setup step - `slope-roof-edges`, the curtain wall jobs, any `--write --setup` proof - prints a warning that is false on the lines that matter: the setup's `level`, `roofType` and `boundary` are called dropped while they build the roof. **Row 71 made the silence speak; this is the speech saying the wrong thing.** | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** Python only, and small: count a value as declared when any writing setup step of the same run declares it, and name the step that takes it. |

| **249** | **A MERGED CHANGE TO A CARD'S IDENTITY NEVER REACHED THE SHARED STORE, SO THE LIVE SEARCH READ THE OLD IDENTITY BESIDE THE NEW PURPOSE.** Found 2026-09-28 while landing PR #351 ([row 5b-236](section-5b-rows-176-200.md)), which changed FRG-VIEW-110's `semantic-identity` in [`apply-color-fill-scheme/fragment.yaml`](../../brain/fragments/apply-color-fill-scheme/fragment.yaml). After the merge, with the main checkout fast-forwarded, the live `heron_lookup("color hvac zones in this plan")` still answered `CREATE_HVAC_ZONE`, the pre-fix answer. A read-only query of the shared store (`%APPDATA%\Heron\knowledge\global.db`, table `fragments`) showed the OLD identity for FRG-VIEW-110 and nothing else stale. **Why:** `heron_brain._Open.__enter__` ([`heron_brain.py`](../../mcp/server/heron_brain.py)) calls `SEARCH.index` and `EMBED.index` on every open, and both re-read a card's purpose and utterances from the FILE - but take its semantic identity, status, capability, domain and risk from the store's ROW. Only `heron_scope.rebuild()` wrote rows, and `_Open` calls it only when the store is empty. So the live search mixed the old identity with the new purpose until that one row was put back by hand (`put_fragment` from the main checkout); the next lookup answered `APPLY_COLOR_FILL_SCHEME`, words #1 and nearness #1. **Not [row 131](section-5-rows-126-150.md)**, which is the store rewritten from whichever worktree indexed last: this is a row nobody rewrote at all. The same class as [row 5b-229](section-5b-rows-176-200.md), which is `check-routing`'s own store and is not touched here. **Reproduced 2026-09-28 on a scratch store** (`HERON_KNOWLEDGE`, trained nearness model): the store built with the pre-#351 card on disk, the card then put back to #351's bytes as a fast-forward would, and the lookup asked again in the same process - the row still held *"colour-fill a plan by one parameter with Revit's colour scheme..."* and the answer was `CREATE_HVAC_ZONE`. | **FIXED in the same change, 2026-09-28, in Python - live in a chat once the Claude app is restarted.** [`heron_scope.refresh()`](../../brain/heron_scope.py), which `_Open` calls before the capability map and both indexes read the rows - and which `heron_retrieve.py` and `heron_context.py` now call too, since their command lines open the same store - rewrites the row of a card whose `fragment.yaml` is not the one the row was made from, when the row differs. It is cheap enough for every lookup: a stat walk says whether any card moved since the store last looked - measured at 21 ms with nothing changed, on a PC busy running every suite - and a digest of the bytes each row was made from lets a reader with no marks read bytes instead of parsing the library. A store written before this, or by another `ROW_FORMAT`, is compared in full once, measured at 5 s. **It never adds or removes a row, and never rewrites a row made from the bytes now on disk**, so a suite's planted DRAFT row, or the one row a session put in by hand to prove an unmerged card, stays as its writer left it. A card that could not be read or parsed is tried again on the next lookup. **Row 131's race:** the SHARED store - the knowledge folder is `%APPDATA%\Heron\knowledge`, however it is spelt, a junction or link included - is refreshed from the MAIN checkout's cards, found through the worktree's `.git` pointer, whichever chat asks. So a merge reaches it on the next lookup from any chat once the main checkout is fast-forwarded, a chat opened before the merge included, and a worktree's unmerged edit never does. A store `HERON_KNOWLEDGE` points somewhere else is private and follows its own checkout; a worktree whose main checkout cannot be found refreshes nothing. The main checkout's cards are read AS that checkout reads them - `heron_fragment.load(folder, root=main)` - because a PROVEN card's proof fingerprint is taken over paths relative to the checkout: measured 2026-09-28 on the main checkout's PROVEN cards, every one failed its own validation read with this worktree's paths and none failed read as the main checkout. **The first version compared the asking checkout's own card with main's** and refreshed only on a match, which left a chat opened before a merge unable to bring the row level; the Codex review of PR #353 found it, with four more that are fixed here (a card that could not be read was never retried, a linked spelling of the shared folder passed for private, the row digest had no format version, the command-line lookups did not refresh). **Measured after the fix**, the same reproduction: private store - the second lookup answered `APPLY_COLOR_FILL_SCHEME` with #351's identity in the row; a stand-in for the shared store (`APPDATA` pointed at a scratch folder) asked from this worktree - the row took #351's identity from the main checkout at the FIRST lookup, while this worktree still held the pre-#351 card, and an unmerged identity written into this worktree's card later left it there. **That first lookup still answered `CREATE_HVAC_ZONE`**, because the searchable purpose and utterances are read from the ASKING checkout's own files - row 131's other half, not touched here: a chat opened before a merge gets the merged identity with its own older purpose until its branch is updated. Pinned in [`tests/test_scope_store.py`](../../tests/test_scope_store.py) section 5, whose new checks go red on `main`'s code, 23 of them, with no crash. **Not measured:** the owner's live store, which was neither rebuilt nor refreshed from here - the first lookup after the restart refreshes it. **Not done here:** a merged NEW card, a REMOVED card, and `rebuild()` run from a worktree into the shared store ([row 5b-233](section-5b-rows-176-200.md)) still need `python brain/heron_scope.py --rebuild` from the main checkout; a card replaced by same-length bytes with its old mtime kept is not seen, the trade `heron_search.disk_status` also makes. **Codex's second review of PR #353 (commit `9e9c4778`) left three P2s the merge did not fix. Fixed 2026-09-28 in the follow-up change.** **(1)** A card the store holds that parsed and did not VALIDATE was still marked as looked at. So a lookup racing a checkout that had written `fragment.yaml` but not yet `tests/cases.yaml`, the impl or the proof never looked at that card again: the files that finish a card do not move its mark. It is left unmarked now, as a card that could not be read already was. A card the store does NOT hold is still marked, or every new card would be parsed on every lookup until a rebuild. **(2)** `python brain/heron_search.py ...` indexed the store without refreshing it first. It refreshes now, as `heron_retrieve.py` and `heron_context.py` do. **(3)** Two chats refreshing the shared store while the main checkout was being fast-forwarded could interleave, because a row and the record of which bytes it came from were committed separately. One chat could write the row from the card BEFORE the fast-forward after the other had recorded the marks and digest of the card AFTER it, and the row stayed stale until that card changed again. Now the cards are read and parsed holding no lock. Then one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction re-reads the records and the rows, stats each card again and leaves it unmarked if it moved since it was read, and writes the rows with `Store.put_row`, which does not commit. It MERGES this reader's marks and digests into the records as they stand at that moment, and commits once. **Measured on a scratch store** (`HERON_KNOWLEDGE`) holding this checkout's whole library, on a PC running every suite beside it: the write lock was held 36 ms when a checkout with no marks read every card's bytes, and 48 ms when a store with no record parsed every card (5.5 s in all). With nothing changed a lookup took 24 ms and never took the lock. Pinned in [`tests/test_scope_store.py`](../../tests/test_scope_store.py) section 5. On `main`'s code four new checks go red with no crash: the mid-checkout card (`[] then []`), the fast-forward race (two checks: the row is left at the card from BEFORE the fast-forward, and the next lookup rewrites nothing) and `heron_search.py`'s wiring. A fifth, that a NEW card which does not validate is parsed once and not on every lookup, is green on `main`, because `main` already did that. It goes red when the fix is widened to leave every card that fails validation unmarked. **The price of (1):** a card the store holds that keeps failing validation is parsed again on every lookup until it is fixed or a rebuild drops it. No card in this checkout does that today: after a full compare, the next refresh found every mark current. Live in a chat once the Claude app is restarted, and only in a chat whose own checkout has this change. A chat started in a worktree runs that worktree's copy, so one whose branch has #353 but not this still runs the old refresh on the shared store, and two of those can still race, until its branch is updated. The owner's live store was neither rebuilt nor refreshed from here. |

| **270** | **THE FAMILY'S OWN SETTINGS AND THE OFFICE'S SHARED PARAMETERS: TWO TOOLS THE OWNER ASKED FOR AFTER A GAP REVIEW.** Built 2026-10-01 - [NEEDS-CHECKING Group BS](../needs-checking/group-bs.md): `set-family-settings`, FRG-DOC-036, and `add-family-shared-parameters`, FRG-PAR-031. The library was searched for the gaps left after Groups BL to BR: nothing set Work Plane-Based, Shared or Always Vertical - and Group BR's own advice is to make a nested family Work Plane-Based - and ADD_FAMILY_PARAMETERS makes family parameters only, which a project's tags and schedules cannot read. **What was decided:** (1) **the shared parameter tool only READS the office's file**, the one Revit is set to, and never sets the path or writes a definition - ADD_PROJECT_PARAMETER does both for a project, but a GUID made here would be a different parameter under the office's name, so a name the file lacks is refused with the close ones; (2) **the kind comes from the file's definition**, so only the scope and the group are asked; (3) **Work Plane-Based is written before Always Vertical**, and anything Revit changes on its own is reported as Revit's rather than as the call's. Also noted on the skill's NOT YET line, as the owner asked: a type catalog, and masking or filled regions inside a family. | **OPEN - built and NOT RUN.** Compiles on 2020 to 2027, `DRAFT`. **For the owner:** BS1 to BS5. |
| **271** | **EVERY HERON LOOKUP STILL COSTS ABOUT 2 SECONDS ON THE OWNER'S PC, NOT THE 0.09 s [04 §8.5](../04-heron-mcp.md#85-one-request-fewer-turns--built-2026-09-30) MEASURED IN A CONTAINER: IT RE-READS EVERY CARD AND EVERY IMPLEMENTATION FILE TO DECIDE NOTHING CHANGED.** Found 2026-10-01 while making a chat's first lookup start sooner ([04 §8.6](../04-heron-mcp.md#86-a-chat-starts-ready--built-2026-10-01)). Profiled on the PC with the store already built and warm, one `heron_lookup` took **2.1 s**: `heron_search.library_digest` 1.08 s and `heron_embed.index` 0.88 s, the search itself 0.08 s. Of that, **1,321 file opens cost 1.06 s** - about 0.8 ms each on this Windows disk, against the ~8 ms for all 429 cards #369 measured on Linux - plus 0.18 s deep-copying 440 cached parses and 0.23 s of `glob` for each folder's `impl/`. Both checks are correctness rules (rows 131, 136, D-61), keyed on bytes on purpose, so this change did **not** touch them. **What would fix it, for whoever takes it:** a per-file fingerprint of `(size, mtime_ns)` from one `os.scandir` walk deciding which files to re-read, the bytes still hashed whenever it moved - which re-opens #369's "content, never the mtime" choice and so is a decision, not a tidy-up. **Not checked:** how much of the 0.8 ms is the antivirus. | **FIXED 2026-10-09 in the code, and NOT VERIFIED on the owner's PC.** Taken on the owner's word of 2026-10-08 - make Heron fast on every PC, and decide - after a new laptop's first family took over seven minutes ([row 378](#)). `heron_fragment.file_mark()` keeps each file's hash with its `(size, mtime_ns, inode)` - and its change time off Windows - and reads the bytes again only when that moved; `heron_search.library_digest` and `heron_fragment.load` both use it, and `library_digest` lists each `impl/` instead of globbing it. **#369's "content, never the mtime" still holds where it matters:** every digest is still a hash of bytes this process read, so a checkout that moves every mtime re-reads and finds nothing changed; a file younger than two seconds is read every time, git's "racily clean" rule, so the same-size rewrite inside one tick that #369 measured is still seen. **What stat cannot see** is a same-size rewrite with its old mtime put back by hand **on Windows** - the gap `heron_scope.cards_on_disk` already accepts for the rows; a new server process or `--rebuild` reads everything. **Measured in a container**, warm store, 480 cards: file opens per lookup **1,440 before, 0 after**; the prepare step 0.135 s before, 0.081 s after (median of five). On the owner's PC the 1,321 opens were 1.06 s of 2.1 s; that saving is **derived, not measured** there. Held by `tests/test_fragment_store.py` section 5, seen to fail on the code before it. |
| **272** | **HERON'S SERVER FROZE FOR GOOD WHEN A CHAT ASKED IT SOMETHING IN ITS FIRST SECONDS WITH REVIT OPEN: TWO THREADS WAITED ON WINDOWS' LOADER LOCK.** Found 2026-10-01 measuring a chat's start ([04 §8.6](../04-heron-mcp.md#86-a-chat-starts-ready--built-2026-10-01)). On `main`, a `heron_lookup` sent the moment the server was ready never answered - **3 runs of 3**, each cut off at 200 s, and a fourth at 300 s - while the same request 8 s later answered in 6-7 s. A stack dump at 40 s: the encoder's warm-up thread inside `create_module` for `numpy._core.multiarray` (Windows loading the DLL), and the event loop inside `threading.Thread.start()`, waiting for the reader thread `heron_bridge_client._read_line` starts when `_revit_version` asks Revit. A thread cannot start while another holds the loader lock, so the whole server - every tool - stopped. It needs Revit open (`discover` pings it), which is why the first runs that evening, with no Revit, never showed it. This is very likely what `heron_embed.warm()`'s 2026-09-06 note recorded as an import "still inside create_module 40 seconds later". **Fixed here:** `heron_embed.warm()` loads numpy on the calling thread before starting its own; three runs of three answered at once (5.3-7.6 s). **Not checked:** whether another native import reached from a background thread can do the same - the re-ranker's torch is the candidate, and is not installed on this PC. | **FIXED - in a real chat, A21.** |
| **273** | **A READ THAT CONSUMES THE CHAIN WAS RESENT WHEN ITS ANSWER WAS LOST, AND THE CHAT HEARD THE RESEND'S REFUSAL FOR A FRAGMENT THAT RAN.** Seen 2026-10-01 on the A20 run ([group A](../needs-checking/group-a.md), PR #380): one `revit_read` with `expect_from` is two entries in the add-in's audit trail - `wf-20261001-155045-0034` ok in 853 ms, `wf-20261001-155045-0035` refused in 0 ms - and one tool call in the Companion. **Mechanism, from the code:** `Bridge.request` resends a request whose answer is lost after sending when it is marked idempotent, and `revit_read` left it at the default (*a read asked twice costs nothing*). That is false for a read with `expectChain`: the first run replaces the chain, so the resend fails `ChainDisagrees` and its *NOTHING WAS BOUND* is the reply. **Why the first answer was lost** is not measured; the code allows one path that fits a fast loss rather than a timeout - `BridgeServer` disposes the active pipe when a NEWER connection arrives, the reply is never written, the client's read returns empty, and the resend goes out on a fresh pipe - so a second connection to that Revit at 18:50:45 is the suspect, not proven. `heron_bridge_client.cmd_fragment` sent `--expect-from` the same way, and sent `--apply` writes idempotent too, against `request()`'s own *Step 6 writes MUST pass False*. **Fixed:** both doors send a consuming run, and the command line a kept write, `idempotent=False`, so a lost answer comes back as `unknown_outcome`; `revit_read` then says the model is unchanged and the producer must run again. Tests: `tests/test_mcp_serves.py` (the stand-in sees `idempotent=False` and the reply wording), `tests/test_chain_expectation.py` (both doors). | **FIXED in code, NOT RUN on a real Revit.** Needs a lost answer arranged on a live bridge - a second connection mid-run - to watch one audit entry and the `unknown_outcome` reply. |
| **274** | **THE COMPANION'S ACTIVITY LIST LABELS FROM THE FIRST LINE'S WORDING, AND GETS TWO KINDS OF REPLY BACKWARDS.** Seen 2026-10-01 on the same A20 run. `Activity.outcome` in `mcp/companion/heron_companion.py` reads only the first line against `REFUSED` and `FAILED` word lists. A words-path hand-back (the candidate list, nothing sent to Revit, the system working as designed) is labelled REFUSED, and the add-in's `expect_from` and binding refusals - whose first line is the capability's name and whose refusal sits further down - are labelled OK. **Not fixed here**, to keep row 273's change reviewable: the honest fix is for each tool to hand the Companion its outcome (`record(..., outcome=...)` already takes one) rather than for the list to guess from prose. | **OPEN.** |

| **277** | **CREATE_FAMILY_REVOLUTION REFUSED A SMALL SPHERE BECAUSE IT READ BACK REVIT'S TESSELLATED BOUNDING BOX.** Seen 2026-10-02 in Revit 2024 on Family1, in the Family Editor (owner Ajmal PS): workPlane `Center (Front/Back)`, profile `1180,748; arc 1212,780 1180,812` (a half-disc of radius 32 against the axis), axisLine `1180,0 1180,2000`, 0 to 360, solid true. Refused with *The revolve reads X 1149.57 to 1210.43 mm, and a full turn of this profile about this axis covers 1148 to 1212 mm*; the same profile at X 1620 failed the same way, while r = 35, 45, 48, 55, 62 and 72 passed in the same session. **Cause:** the full-turn read-back in `brain/fragments/create-family-revolution/impl/any/fragment.cs` compared `form.get_BoundingBox(null)`, which Revit builds from the form's tessellation, so a curved extreme reads inside the true surface. The r = 32 sphere read 1.57 mm short each side against an allowance of 1 mm + 0.5 % = 1.32 mm. **The larger spheres passed by luck, measured 2026-10-02** on Family1 with a temporary finding printing the box, every run rolled back: the box read X exactly for r = 20, 35, 45, 72 and 500, and 1.20, 1.38, 1.55, 1.57 and 1.53 mm short for r = 48, 55, 62, 32 and 150. The shortfall does not grow with the radius - it stays near 1.2 to 1.6 mm and turns on where Revit's facet corners fall against the axes - so r = 48, 55 and 62 passed the old allowance by 0.28, 0.17 and 0.07 mm, and r = 32 missed it by 0.25. **Fixed:** the extent is measured on the solid itself - every edge walked at 257 points, every face sampled on its own surface at 129 x 65 points inside its trim (`Face.Evaluate`, `Face.IsInside`, both in 2020) - and the allowance tightened to 0.5 mm + 0.1 % of the size, which the sampling step stays inside (under 0.06 %) and a profile on the wrong axes does not. A form with no faces to measure falls back to the box and the old allowance. The `built` extent reports the measured numbers. Case added to `tests/cases.yaml`. Three deliberate breaks on the `main` checkout each turned the new suite red: the line speaking there, a PROVEN count wrong on this repository alone, and a 6 s delay on this repository alone. The last two are what the old suite could not see on `main`, where the line it ran did no work. |
| **280** | **EVERY OTHER KIND OF FAMILY: ELEVEN TOOLS, ONE WIDENED, SEVEN SKILLS - AND WHY A PART THAT SWINGS ABOUT A REFERENCE LINE IS STILL NOT BUILT.** Built 2026-10-02 on the owner's request of 2026-10-01 for *"line based, ceiling based, patterns, floor based, wall based, all kind"* of family creation, after six research passes and a reading of every Revit member used in all eight releases' reference assemblies - [43](../43-building-revit-families.md), [NEEDS-CHECKING Group BV](../needs-checking/group-bv.md). **New:** `report-family-template` (FRG-DOC-037), `report-family-type-catalog` (FRG-DOC-038), `place-adaptive-points`, `draw-family-point-curves`, `create-conceptual-form`, `create-family-host-opening`, `set-family-sweep-profile` (FRG-GEO-050 to 054), `add-family-type-parameter` (FRG-PAR-032), `draw-family-filled-region`, `add-family-flip-control`, `draw-family-detail-lines` (FRG-VIEW-117 to 119); **widened before any proof:** `set-family-settings` from four switches to eleven settings, Part Type and Profile Usage among them; **skills:** `hosted-`, `line-based-`, `pattern-based-`, `adaptive-`, `profile-`, `detail-` and `mep-fitting-family-creation`, and `family-creation` widened. **What was decided:** (1) **the template's kind is READ first, never guessed** - a hosted family cannot be changed into another kind, so every skill starts with the report; (2) **release-bound calls are found by signature** - a filled region in a 3D family (2023), a masking region (2024) - and an older release REFUSES by name with what to do instead; (3) **a label is never promised** - Revit's API cannot make one on any release 2020 to 2027 (2027 only reads them), so `detail-family-creation` says so before starting; (4) **an API-made opening is fixed-size and says so** - its sketch cannot be locked through the API; (5) **a type catalog's header words come from Revit's own `GetTypeCatalogString...` calls**, never typed, because Revit renamed unit words in 2021; (6) **a Family Type parameter takes its value BEFORE it is linked**, the order a published Autodesk answer gives round a linking defect, and reports which of a nested copy's parameters Revit took. **Left NOT YET, with the evidence:** a part that rotates about a REFERENCE LINE. The line can be made (`ModelCurve.ChangeToReferenceLine`) and an angle labelled, but no documented call returns a reference to one of the line's own planes; the only route published - a forum user's trial, reported by The Building Coder - builds one from the line's stable representation plus a numeric suffix, a string Autodesk's own remarks on `ConvertToStableRepresentation` say is not meant to be parsed. Heron does not build on an undocumented string; the `family-creation` skill's NOT YET line says so. | **OPEN - built and NOT RUN.** Compiles on 2020 to 2027, every tool `DRAFT`. **For the owner:** BV1 to BV16. |

Autodesk Help's own words (*"the value to find in the first, second ... columns"*) can be read either way. **Both are recorded and neither is edited**: the fragment is PROVEN, and a changed card is a claim that needs the run.
