| **284** | **THE RE-CHECK READ ROW 280'S OWN CODE AGAIN, AND FOUND FOURTEEN DEFECTS BEFORE ANY OF IT HAD RUN - ALL FIXED; AND TWO TOOLS WIDENED TO THE LAST TWO NOT YET ROWS WITH DOCUMENTED CALLS.** Found 2026-10-02: four independent reads of the twelve tools of row 280, each call checked against the RevitAPI XML of all eight releases. **Fixed:** (1) `create-conceptual-form` took an extrusion's direction from curves never put in order - edges drawn `1,2 \| 3,2 \| 4,3 \| 1,4` gave NO direction and a false "encloses no area", and the side a depth went depended on how the lines were drawn; the curves are now chained end to end and the side is a RULE (the axis the profile faces most is positive - up for a flat panel), reported; (2) a closed circle or ellipse as a profile reached `GetEndPoint` and died with Revit's raw error - it now closes by itself and takes its own normal; (3) "not flat" and "no area" were THROWN after nothing had been checked, now refused by name; (4) it made forms in any family - it now reads the three conceptual flags first; (5) `place-adaptive-points` read placement numbers back and never checked them - they must now rise in the order given; (6) a point where one already was in the FAMILY was not refused; (7) a mass reported "0 placement points"; (8) `add-family-flip-control` offered elevations, which `NewControl`'s own remark refuses - a floor or ceiling plan now, the floor plan where both share a name; (9) its card said a single arrow flips left-right - Autodesk's remark on `Control` says a single arrow ROTATES 180° and a double one MIRRORS; card, finding and BV3 now say so; (10) "0, -400" was refused as "not a control shape"; (11) `draw-family-detail-lines` never read its line style back, and named "the family's category" for "none" without reading it - a named subcategory is now offered only where the line's own `GetLineStyleIds` lists it (`CurveElement.LineStyle`'s remark ties line styles to the Lines category), set and read back, and "none" reports Revit's default by the name it reads; (12) `report-family-template` showed text and element parameters blank (`AsValueString` is null for them), and listed the browser panes as views; (13) `add-family-type-parameter` refused the group "Other" on 2022 to 2027 (the empty `ForgeTypeId`), reused a parameter of the same name and swapped every part it already drove, and failed misleadingly on "Small" and "small"; (14) `set-family-sweep-profile` read the flip before the profile was set, and its flip test used a symmetric profile that cannot show a flip. **Widened:** `place-adaptive-points` to points HOSTED on a line at a ratio (`NewPointOnEdge`) or on a point's own plane (`PointOnPlane.NewPointOnPlane`), and `create-conceptual-form` to a SWEEP or swept blend along a path (`NewSweptBlendForm`) - together, the two-point adaptive beam row 280 left NOT YET. **A third reading - the Codex review on the pull request, 2026-10-02 - found six more, fixed the same way:** (15) `add-family-type-parameter` reused a parameter of the same name without comparing its scope, so an instance one asked for could quietly be a type one - now refused; (16) `set-family-connector-roles` told the modeller to write "A > none" in the same call as "A > C", then refused the two as one connector in two pairs, and its read-back would have failed the unlink - one unlink beside one link of the same connector is now allowed, the unlinks running first; (17) `create-conceptual-form` reported a revolve's extent from the first form Revit returned, not from all of them; (18) `report-family-template` called any family with `IsCurtainPanelFamily` set pattern-based - the flag alone is not trusted to tell it from a classic curtain wall panel, so reference points, which only a conceptual family holds, decide it, and a classic panel is named as one; (19) it did not report a structural family's Material for Model Behavior and Section Shape, the two settings `set-family-settings` now takes; (20) the `line-based-family-creation` skill and [43 §4](../43-building-revit-families.md) wrote the array-count guard as `if(n < 2, 2, n)`, with an `n` no Revit formula defines. **Three it raised were checked and left as they are:** `NewPointOnPlane`'s fourth argument is documented as the point's X-coordinate vector *in the plane's coordinates*, so `XYZ.BasisX` is each plane's own X, not a direction along the YZ plane's normal (BV19 runs exactly that case); the reference assemblies give `InitialIlluminanceIntensity(double distance, double illuminance)`, the order `set-family-light-source` passes; and the typed totals it asked to remove are a convention `main` already carries, checked by `check-docs` in README - the gap behind it, two wordings no gate reads, is row 288. **A fourth reading - Codex's second review, of those fixes, the same day - found six more, fixed the same way:** (21) `create-conceptual-form`, `place-adaptive-points` and `divide-family-surface` still admitted any family with `IsCurtainPanelFamily` set, so a classic curtain wall panel met a failed call instead of a refusal - they now ask for reference points too, the test `report-family-template` reads; (22) `create-conceptual-form` turned a depth under 0.5 mm into a surface without a word - only empty or 0 means a surface now, and a thinner depth is refused; (23) `report-family-template` tested the broad annotation category type before Detail Items and Profiles - each named category now comes first, whichever type Revit gives them; (24) `set-family-light-source` read back a web's IES file but not its tilt - it now compares the tilt, in the degrees the reference gives, and reports it. **And two in the job files, which would have cost a real run:** (25) the two READ reports' negative cases were other families, whose content the runner's judge reads as NEG NOT EMPTY - they now run in a project, where each says it is not a family and returns nothing else; (26) the beam's form job was to sweep points the first job places, which the runner rolls back and gives no pause between - it now has a family of its own whose points and loop are made before the run and kept, and `family-kinds-2026-10-02.yaml` and `family-recheck-2026-10-02.yaml` say the same of their own arrangements. **A fifth reading - Codex's third review - found one more, fixed:** (27) the `detail-family-creation` skill had the modeller link a part's Visible to a Yes/No by hand, though LINK_FAMILY_PARAMETER does it - named in the step and declared in its `needs` now. **And it questioned one thing, which was checked:** that a Generic Model Pattern Based family is not a curtain-panel family to Revit. Revit's remark on `DividedSurface` takes "a FamilySymbol element from a Curtain Panel family" as a divided surface's type, which is what such a family is, so the flag should read true for it; the report now names the category rather than calling every one a curtain panel, and BV1 records what the flag reads. **A sixth reading - Codex's fourth review - found three more in the tools and a gap in the skills, all fixed:** (28) `set-family-light-source` set a light's shape or distribution STYLE, which Revit keeps for the whole family, even with one type named, so the other types changed unasked - a change of style with one type named is now refused; (29) `draw-family-detail-lines` took a profile's loops one by one, so two that crossed or touched were drawn - every edge is now tested against the other loops' before anything is drawn; (30) it locked a line to EVERY named plane it lay on, so an edge plane over a template's centre plane got two locks that a label would pull apart - it now locks to the one that does not define the origin and refuses two such planes as ambiguous; (31) three skills had steps using tools their `needs` did not declare, and an audit of every family skill found more: steps that point at FAMILY-CREATION's step 7, 8 or 12 or load a family into a test project now declare the tools those steps use, in `hosted-`, `line-based-`, `pattern-based-`, `adaptive-`, `profile-`, `detail-`, `door-window-` and `lighting-fixture-family-creation`. **A seventh reading - Codex's fifth review - found three more, fixed:** (32) `draw-family-detail-lines` tested a profile's loops against each other but not one loop against itself, so a loop that crossed itself was drawn - its edges that do not share a corner are now tested too; (33) `place-adaptive-points` let a mass ask for placement or shape handle points, which Revit's remark on `MakeAdaptivePoint` makes only in an adaptive family, so the call failed half-made - a mass, or a panel Revit does not read as adaptive, is now refused them up front; (34) the lighting skill said HOSTED-FAMILY-CREATION's steps hold for a recessed fixture without declaring their host opening - the ROOT CAUSE of findings 31 and this one is a skill saying another's steps "still hold" while `needs` is a flat list with no inheritance, so the six skills that say so - hosted, line-based, mep-fitting, structural, door-window and lighting-fixture - now declare every capability FAMILY-CREATION's own `needs` lists, and the last two HOSTED-FAMILY-CREATION's as well. | **FIXED in code, NOT RUN.** Compiles on 2020 to 2027. **For the owner:** BV1, BV3, BV4, BV6, BV8, BV10, BV11, BV13, BV17, BV18, BV19, BV20 and BV22 carry the new expectations, and the three job files their new arrangements. |
| **285** | **`SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` COUNTS A SETTING REVIT CHANGED ON ITS OWN AS ASKED AND AS CHANGED, AND DOES NOT MARK IT AS REVIT'S.** Found 2026-10-02 by the re-check of row 284, in counting code that predates row 280's widening - so recorded, not fixed (AGENTS.md). `brain/fragments/set-family-settings/impl/any/fragment.cs`: "already set" is judged AFTER the earlier writes, "changed" against the snapshot taken BEFORE any. Asked `Work Plane-Based=no; Always Vertical=yes` from on and off: if writing the first makes Revit turn the second on by itself, the second is counted as already set AND as changed - "2 changed and 1 already as asked" for two requests - and its row carries no "(Revit's own change)" tag, which the card promises. **Whether Revit flips Always Vertical on its own is unproven**; NEEDS-CHECKING BS2 runs that pair. **Fix when owned:** count `changed` only for the settings actually written, and tag every other setting that moved as Revit's. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** For the owner: BS2 shows whether it can happen. |
| **286** | **THE RE-CHECK'S COVERAGE AUDIT: [43 §1](../43-building-revit-families.md) STILL SAID STRUCTURAL FRAMING WAS "NOT COVERED HERE", AND DOORS, WINDOWS, CURTAIN PANELS, RAILINGS, MASSES AND LIGHTING FIXTURES HAD NO SECTION.** Found 2026-10-02 when the owner asked *"is that everything covered? ... check everything once more"*: every family kind and parametric technique was mapped to a skill, a tool or a NOT YET row, and these were the gaps. Two more research passes - search extracts again, every page but GitHub refused for direct fetch, so each fact is marked SOURCED, UNSURE or not found - wrote them into 43 §6, §7, §10 and §12; **three skills** were added for the kinds a modeller builds most and Heron's tools can serve: `door-window-family-creation`, `structural-family-creation` and `lighting-fixture-family-creation`, and `family-creation`'s step 0 points to them. **Left NOT YET, in 43 §11, with the reason:** Divide Surface and a revolve form in a conceptual family (calls exist, not wrapped); a structural family's Material for Model Behavior, Symbolic Representation and Section Shape (built-in parameters exist, `SET_FAMILY_SETTINGS` does not take them); editing a template's own sketch - a beam's section, a door's opening - which no Heron tool does, so a new form locked to the planes is the route. | **OPEN - written and NOT RUN.** Every skill `DRAFT`; the owner's runs of Group BV are their proof. |
| **287** | **THE LAST NOT YET ROWS WITH DOCUMENTED CALLS, BUILT - DIVIDE SURFACE, A CONCEPTUAL REVOLVE, AND A STRUCTURAL FAMILY'S MATERIAL FOR MODEL BEHAVIOR AND SECTION SHAPE.** Found 2026-10-02 by the coverage audit of row 286, which left them in [43 §11](../43-building-revit-families.md) with calls that exist on all eight releases. **New:** `divide-family-surface` (FRG-GEO-055) - `DividedSurface.Create` on a form's face chosen by the way it faces, each direction a Number or a fixed distance through `SpacingRule.SetLayoutFixedNumber`/`SetLayoutFixedDistance` (whose remarks ask for justification, rotation and offset together), and optionally a tile pattern or a loaded panel family from the surface's own `GetValidTypes`. **Widened:** `create-conceptual-form` to a REVOLVE about one straight line (`NewRevolveForms`, which may return several forms - all are read back); `set-family-settings` to Material for Model Behavior (`StructuralMaterialType`) and Section Shape (the enum `Family.StructuralSectionShape` carries, read from that property because its namespace is not one the executor imports). **What was decided:** whether Revit's Number counts cells or lines is NOT promised - the gridline count is reported and BV21 records it; **Symbolic Representation stays by hand**, because no source gave the values its parameter stores. **What remains NOT YET after this row is only what Revit's API cannot do or documents no route for**: a label, a pattern-based panel's tile pattern, an opening's or a template's sketch, a reference line's planes - and what the modeller does by hand: the Light Source tick, a wattage until BV18. | **OPEN - built and NOT RUN.** Compiles on 2020 to 2027. **For the owner:** BV21 to BV23. |
| **288** | **THE GATE THAT CHECKS THE LIBRARY'S COUNTS READS ONE WORDING, AND TWO OTHERS GO STALE UNSEEN: `brain/README.md`'S "N FRAGMENTS ARE `PROVEN`; THE OTHER M", AND EVERY COUNT OF SKILLS.** Found 2026-10-02 answering the Codex review of the family pull request, which asked for the typed totals to go - so recorded, not fixed (AGENTS.md). **A mutation test on a scratch copy of the branch:** `README.md`'s PROVEN total - that day 272, of the 464 fragments - changed to 270 makes `tools/check-docs.py` exit 1; but `brain/README.md`'s "272 fragments are `PROVEN`; the other 192" changed to 270 and 194, or "twenty-four skills" changed to "twenty-two" in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `brain/README.md` or `docs/README.md`, leaves it at 0 - and `tests/test_library_total.py`, `test_skill_catalog.py`, `test_docs_guard.py` and `test_agent_count.py` pass each change too. **It has already happened:** at `main` aeff14e, `brain/README.md` read "306 fragments are `PROVEN`; the other 120" while the checked README sentence beside it gave 272 and 178; the family pull request re-derived it with the rest. **Fix when owned:** either those sentences become checked claims - check-docs §6 taught "N fragments are PROVEN; the other M", and a count of skills derived from `brain/skills/*.yaml` the way `python brain/heron_skill.py` prints it - or the typed totals go and each place names the command, as AGENTS.md asks. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** For the owner: which of the two. |
| **289** | **NO TOOL DRAWS A ROOM SEPARATION LINE, SO A ROOM WITH AN OPEN ARCHWAY CANNOT BE SPLIT.** Found 2026-10-02 drawing the owner's shop-and-flat plan in Project2 (Revit 2024): the kitchen and the living room are joined by a 900 mm opening, and PLACE_ROOM_AT_POINT made one room of both because nothing bounds the gap. `heron_lookup "draw a room separation line across an opening"` returned CREATE_ROOM_ELEVATIONS, and no fragment under `brain/fragments/` calls `NewRoomBoundaryLines`. Every plan with an open kitchen, an archway or a lobby hits this. **Fix when owned:** a fragment that draws room separation lines along given points on a level (`Document.Create.NewRoomBoundaryLines` with a `SketchPlane` and a `CurveArray`), and the matching space separation for MEP (`NewSpaceBoundaryLines`). | **OPEN - recorded, not built.** |
| **290** | **NAMING ROOMS NEEDS THEIR ELEMENT IDS, AND THE REPLY THAT LISTS THEM SHOWS ONLY THREE.** Found 2026-10-02 in Project2: 5 rooms were placed and `created` printed `352546, 352548, 352549, ...`. To name them, IMPORT_PARAMETER_VALUES needs an id per row, so the last two ids were GUESSED (352550 to 352553, all named Shop); the import wrote 5 and named the 2 spare rows unmatched, so nothing went wrong - but only because both shops had one name. The three-item cut is the reply formatter's (see the memory and the earlier rows on truncated lists); this row is the job it blocks. **Fix when owned:** either a fragment that names a room by its number or by a point inside it, or the central reply fix that prints a full id list when a later step needs it. | **OPEN - recorded, not built.** |
| **291** | **A FAMILY BUILT FROM A PHOTO TAKES ONE CALL PER SHAPE.** Found 2026-10-02 in Family1: a minibus took 42 forms plus planes, a cut and 10 colour calls; a helicopter 23 forms and 7 colour calls - about 100 round trips for two objects, each one a separate undo entry. Every form is a CREATE_FAMILY_EXTRUSION or CREATE_FAMILY_REVOLUTION with values the caller already had in one list. **Fix when owned:** one fragment that takes a list of forms (kind, plane, profile, range, material) and builds them in one transaction, reading each back as the single-form fragments do - or a documented chain that does the same. Measure the turn time before and after (see where-heron-time-goes). | **OPEN - recorded, not built.** |
| **292** | **A TYPE NAME IS PRINTED ONE WAY AND ACCEPTED ANOTHER: SELECT_TYPES WRITES `M_Single-Flush : 0915 x 2134mm`, AND PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY REFUSES IT.** Found 2026-10-02 in Project2 (Revit 2024). SELECT_TYPES' `matchedNames` gave `Family : Type` with a space each side of the colon. Passing that as `symbol` to PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY answered *No family type called "M_Single-Flush : 0915 x 2134mm"* six times; `M_Single-Flush: 0915 x 2134mm` (no space before the colon) worked at once. One part of Heron hands out a name another part will not take. **Fix when owned:** the type resolver accepts either spacing round the colon (a name may not contain a colon, so this is safe), or SELECT_TYPES prints the form the resolver takes. Check every printer of `Family : Type`. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** |
| **293** | **PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY REPORTS EVERY WINDOW AS `wrongHinge` - "NO SWING ARC".** Found 2026-10-02 in Project2: three `M_Fixed` windows placed into their walls, facing their rooms (`wrongRoom` 0), and all three came back in `wrongHinge` with *no swing arc was found for it in any plan*. A window has no swing arc, so the hinge check does not apply to it and should not be counted as a fault. **Fix when owned:** skip the hinge check (and say so once) when the symbol's category is Windows, or when the caller gave no hinge point. | **FIXED 2026-10-06 at DRAFT, in PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY version 4 (PR #423) - not yet signed.** A hinge is read for a DOOR alone now, and the summary says once that a window draws no swing arc. The same edit stopped version 3 turning every window to face its room (row 5b-325): a window keeps its facing along its wall's exterior. Measured in Project2 (Revit 2024, session 51820), rolled back, on two M_Fixed 0915 x 1220mm windows: version 3 on `main` named both in `wrongHinge` (*no swing arc*) and flipped both to face the room; version 4 on the same walls named neither, and read both back facing out, FacingFlipped False. The proof record waits for the owner's signature (`brain/proof-drafts/runs/place-hosted-family-window.json`, beside the skylight's). |
| **294** | **PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY PLACES A DOOR IT CANNOT CHECK WHEN THE PLAN HAS NO ROOMS, AND SAYS SO ONLY AFTERWARDS.** Found 2026-10-02 in Project2: three doors went in before any room existed, each kept with `wrongRoom` *no room at the point it should open into*. Placing the rooms and re-running the same values then flipped two of the three (`flipped` 1, `handFlipped` 1, `toRoomSwapped` 1 each), so the first run had left two doors swinging the wrong way. **Fix when owned:** decide the facing from the geometry (which side of the wall `toRoomAt` lies) when no room is there, and set To Room only when rooms exist; or refuse with *place the rooms first*. Either way the first run should not keep a door it knows it cannot check without saying so up front. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** |
| **295** | **REPORT_FAMILY_FORMS READS A SOLID WITH A VOID CUT IN IT AS 0 L.** Found 2026-10-02 in Family1 (Revit 2024): the minibus body (an extrusion) was cut by a void with COMBINE_FAMILY_FORMS, which itself reported *33288.157 L before, 31796.287 L after*. REPORT_FAMILY_FORMS `which=all` then listed the same solid as `0 L`, while every uncut form read its real volume. The volume is probably read from geometry that a combined form no longer exposes the same way. **Fix when owned:** read the volume of a form inside a `GeometryCombination` from the combination's solid, or print *in a combination - see its volume* rather than a 0 that reads as empty. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** |
| **296** | **CREATE_FAMILY_EXTRUSION SENDS LOOPS THAT TOUCH EACH OTHER, AND REVIT ANSWERS ONLY "UNEXPECTED INTERNAL ERROR: CODE 1".** Found 2026-10-01 in Family1 building a transformer and a launch pad: a rectangular ring drawn as four rectangles sharing edges, and grid strips crossing a spine in one profile, each failed with that message and nothing else. Drawing the ring as an outer rectangle with an inner one (a hole), and the crossing strips as their own extrusion, worked. **Fix when owned:** before sending, test the loops for shared edges or crossings and refuse naming the two loops that touch, with the hint to draw a ring as outer plus inner. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** |
| **297** | **ADD_FAMILY_CONNECTOR (FRG-MEP-053) REFUSES REVIT'S OWN NAME FOR A FIRE PROTECTION SYSTEM.** Found 2026-10-03 in Family2 (a Sprinklers family, Revit 2024, session 41260): `system=Fire Protection Wet` was refused with *No connector system is called "Fire Protection Wet"*, and `system=FireProtectWet` made *pipe connector, Fire Protect Wet*. The fragment matched only the `PipeSystemType` enum names with the spaces taken out, and the enum says `FireProtect...` where Revit's System Classification list says **Fire Protection Wet, Fire Protection Dry, Fire Protection Pre-Action, Fire Protection Other** - so a modeller typing what Revit shows is refused, and the refusal offered no close name because neither squashed name contains the other. The two hydronic systems differ the same way (enum `SupplyHydronic`, shown *Hydronic Supply*). **Fixed in the fragment, DRAFT:** each system is matched by its enum name AND by the name Revit displays for it (a table of the six that differ, used only when that enum value exists in the release running); the report and the refusal print the displayed name, and the refusal's list of accepted names now includes the fire protection ones. Pipe "Other" is NOT taken as Other Pipe: a duct has Other Air, and a bare "Other" would pick the kind for the modeller. **Proof owed:** on a real family, `Fire Protection Wet` makes a pipe connector reading Fire Protection Wet in Properties, and `Fire Protection Wett` is refused naming the fire protection systems. | **OPEN - fixed in source 2026-10-03; the fragment stays DRAFT until proved on a real model.** |
| **298** | **PLACE_ROOM_AT_POINT (FRG-GEO-029) CALLS EVERY SPACE IT PLACES "NOT ENCLOSED".** Found 2026-10-03 in the unsaved Revit 2024 project Project2 (session 41260): placing rooms at 37 and then 42 points returned `unenclosed 0`, but placing MEP spaces at the SAME points (`asSpace=true`) returned `unenclosed 37` and `unenclosed 42` - every space. Straight afterwards SELECT_IN_REGION found each space with a bounding box, and REPORT_ROOM_SPACE_DATA on Space 2 (id 353348) read it as enclosed (only *volume not computed*, because the project's volume computation is off). The fragment read `Area` in the same breath as `NewSpace`, before Revit had regenerated, so every space read 0 - the same false zero PLACE_ROOMS found and fixed on 2026-09-13. The fragment's own proof of 2026-09-13 recorded `unenclosed 5` of 5 rooms, which this probably explains too. **Measured the same day on the same model** - Project2 saved as *c bulding test* (79 rooms, 79 spaces), Revit 2024, session 41260 - against an 8000 x 6000 mm box of four `Generic - 200mm` walls built on the empty Roof level 300 m from the building and deleted afterwards on the owner's word, every run rolled back: one point inside the box and one outside gave `created 2, unenclosed 1` naming only the outside one, and the inside point alone gave `unenclosed 0` - for rooms AND for spaces. The OLD code on the same box gave `unenclosed 2` and `unenclosed 1` for spaces: the inside space too, which is the defect reproduced. `heron_validate accept` wants either an empty negative leg, which this fragment cannot give (it always places something), or a D-53 tracking set, and `validate --vary` split its values on commas, which every point contains - so **`validate --vary` and `tools/prove-tracking.py` now split on a pipe (the vertical bar) when a value list has one**, and the D-53 tracking run followed: 1, 2 and 3 points on Roof made 1, 2 and 3 spaces, rolled back. **Signed by Ajmal PS and promoted back to PROVEN on 2026-10-03, on his approval after the runs passed.** **Fixed in the fragment:** every point is placed first, the document is regenerated once, and only then is each room or space measured. **The code changed inside a PROVEN `impl/`, so the fingerprint moved and the fragment is DRAFT again, its 2026-09-13 proof kept in the card as the record of the old code. Proof owed** ([Group BW](../needs-checking/group-bw.md)) on a real model: points inside enclosed areas give `unenclosed 0` for rooms AND for spaces, and a point outside any enclosure is named in `unenclosed`, for both. | **FIXED 2026-10-03 - proved on Project2 and PROVEN again.** |
| **299** | **NO FRAGMENT COULD MAKE A STAIR, WHATEVER IT WAS WRITTEN TO DO: REVIT STARTS A STAIR'S EDIT MODE ONLY WITH NO TRANSACTION OPEN, AND EVERY WRITE FRAGMENT RUNS INSIDE ONE.** Found 2026-10-03 when the school chat on Project2 asked *"make the stair also"* and nothing in the library made stairs. Measured on Project2, Revit 2024, session 41260, rolled back: `StairsEditScope.IsPermitted` read false inside the add-in's transaction and `Start` threw *not permitted to start ... the document is currently modifiable*. **What was decided:** [D-112](../decisions/D-112.md) (Proposed) - a fragment declaring the ambient need `editScopeFailures` runs inside the job's TransactionGroup with no transaction open, opens its own edit scope and transactions, and gives each of them Heron's failure discipline; never on the read path. Built on it: `create-stairs` (FRG-ELE-076), straight or U inside a plan rectangle, riser and tread from the stair type, all or nothing, and optionally the opening in the top level's slab, read back by the slab's volume. **Also measured:** a SHAFT opening from Level 1 to Level 2 over the hall removed nothing from either slab inside a transaction, while the slab's own opening removed 8 m3 - so the stair cuts the slab, not a shaft. **Found on the way, recorded:** `tools/check-routing.py` run once without `HERON_KNOWLEDGE` rebuilt the shared store from this branch; it was back to origin/main's 464 rows when checked minutes later. | **OPEN - built and NOT RUN.** Compiles on 2020 to 2027, `DRAFT`. **For the owner:** [Group BX](../needs-checking/group-bx.md) - deploy the add-in, restart Revit, BX1 first. |
| **300** | **LOAD_FAMILY HANGS BEHIND REVIT'S OWN DIALOG WHEN THE FILE IS FROM A LATER REVIT.** Found 2026-10-03 loading `Catch_Basin new.rfa` into Family1 (Revit 2024, session 32340): Revit opened its modal *"The file Catch_Basin new.rfa was saved in a later version of Revit and cannot be retrieved in this version"*, the call answered `unknown_outcome`, then `revit_busy`, then `still_running` for over 90 s, and nothing moved until the dialog was closed by hand. A modeller sees a frozen Heron. **Fix when owned:** read the file's saved release with `BasicFileInfo.Extract` before `LoadFamily`, and refuse a later release in words, naming it; or answer Revit's dialog through `DialogBoxShowing` and turn it into a refusal. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** |
| **301** | **PLACE_NESTED_FAMILY THROWS WHEN THE NESTED FAMILY CARRIES ITS OWN OFFSET.** Found 2026-10-03 in Family1 (Revit 2024): `Round void : HWC 10 cm` (Revit's Structural Precast library, Work Plane-Based) on `Ref. Level` at `0,-1000,0` threw *"The placed family stands at 0, -1000, -200 mm, not 0, -1000, 0 mm"* and rolled back. Revit put it where the family's own offset says, 200 mm below its plane; the read-back calls that a failure. `Solid Wall - Bushing` and `M_People Content in Route Analysis` placed and read back cleanly at the same point. **Fix when owned:** compare the point ON the host plane (the location minus the offset along the plane's normal), and report the family's own offset in `placed` rather than failing. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** |
| **302** | **ADD_FAMILY_TYPE_PARAMETER FINDS NOTHING IT CAN LINK, ON EITHER ROUTE, IN REVIT 2024.** Found 2026-10-03 in Family1: a placed copy of `Solid Wall - Bushing` (one type) and of `M_People Content in Route Analysis` (two types) were each refused *"has no type or profile Revit lets be linked to a family parameter"*; a sweep drawn with the loaded profile `Support Profile : Support Profile` was refused the same way. With the `CanElementParameterBeAssociated` gate removed in a scratch copy, the nested copy still found no `ElementId` carrier among `ELEM_FAMILY_AND_TYPE_PARAM`, `ELEM_TYPE_PARAM`, `SYMBOL_ID_PARAM`, and the sweep's `PROFILE_FAM_TYPE` was refused by Revit itself: *"This parameter cannot be associated"*. **Measured further the same day, rolled back:** with a Family Type parameter of the nested family's own category made first (Structural Connections, Generic Models, Profiles) AND its current-type value set to the type the copy shows - docs/43's reported way round Autodesk's linking defect - `AssociateElementParameterToFamilyParameter` still answered *"This parameter cannot be associated"* for `ELEM_TYPE_LABEL` (the Label the Properties palette shows), `ELEM_FAMILY_AND_TYPE_PARAM`, `ELEM_TYPE_PARAM` and `ELEM_FAMILY_PARAM` on both copies, and for the sweep's `Profile`; `CanElementParameterBeAssociated` was false for every one before and after. So on Revit 2024 the API refuses the link the Label list makes by hand; nothing in the fragment's choice of parameter would change that. Stays DRAFT. **Fix when owned:** run the same probe on Revit 2027 (and 2020); if one release accepts it, gate the fragment to it and prove there, and if none does, take the capability out of the library in favour of a modeller step the reply names. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** |
| **303** | **DIVIDE_FAMILY_SURFACE PRINTS EACH GRID WITH THE OTHER DIRECTION'S LINE COUNT.** Found 2026-10-03 in Family5 (a Mass, Revit 2024) on a 3000 x 2000 x 2500 mm form: top face `uGrid=20 vGrid=4` read *"U number 20, 7 gridline(s); V number 4, 23 gridline(s)"*; front face `uGrid=3 vGrid=1000 mm` read 6 and 6; `uGrid=6 vGrid=500 mm` read 9 and 9. The grid follows the input - the counts move with it - but `NumberOfUGridlines` counts lines running along U, which the V rule makes, so a modeller reading "U number 20, 7 gridlines" is told something false. The fragment's own finding already defers the division-or-line question to NEEDS-CHECKING BV21. **Fix when owned:** pair the U rule with `NumberOfVGridlines` and the V rule with `NumberOfUGridlines`, or word each as the lines it makes, and settle BV21 by eye on one surface. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** |

| **308** | **NO TOOL SWITCHED A FAMILY PARAMETER BETWEEN TYPE AND INSTANCE, SO A FAMILY BUILT ON TYPE SIZES COULD NOT BE MADE TO FOLLOW ITS PIPE.** Found 2026-10-04 by Ajmal on his PPR gate valve (TRG_PLMB_VLV_PPR Gate Valve_GV_R0, Revit 2024): its 36 size parameters are TYPE parameters - Nominal Diameter drives the connectors and 35 are formulas off it - so the valve does not resize to the pipe it sits on, while the two PPR check valves, built with INSTANCE parameters, do. Revit has `FamilyManager.MakeInstance` and `MakeType` on every release 2020 to 2027; nothing in the library called them, and ADD_FAMILY_PARAMETERS told the modeller to change scope by hand in Family Types. **Fixed by widening ADD_FAMILY_PARAMETERS (FRG-PAR-022) to version 2**, not by a new tool: `switchExisting` true switches named parameters, or `all`, to the scope `instance` names. A type formula cannot read an instance parameter, so the order is worked out from the formulas (to instance: what reads, before what is read; to type: the reverse), and a switch that would leave a type formula reading an instance parameter is refused naming it, before anything changes. A parameter a nested family's TYPE parameter is linked to, a type catalogue column and a built-in are refused by name too. Every parameter is read back - scope, formula, associated connector and nested parameters, labelled dimensions - all or nothing. | **FIXED - DRAFT until signed (D-30).** Measured on scratch Family17 (Revit 2024, session 8804, unsaved, made for it): all to instance switched C, B, A and read back with the connector on A and the label on B kept; A alone refused naming B; back to type switched A, B, C; C alone refused naming C. Compiles on 2020 to 2027. Group CA owes the signature, the nested-link and catalogue refusals on a real family, and 2020. |

The panel says "two undo entries" beside the button and in its answer. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** One entry needs both writes inside one TransactionGroup, which is an add-in change (a request that carries two fragments, or a group the bridge opens around a chain) - not something the server can do by calling revit_change twice. Until then the modeller undoes twice, and the second write runs only if the first was kept. Group [CC](../needs-checking/group-cc.md) row CC11 checks the two entries on a real model. |
| **315** | **A YES/NO COLUMN EXPORTED BY HERON COULD NOT BE IMPORTED BACK BY HERON.** Found 2026-10-04 by reading the code during the [docs/45](../45-five-more-downloaded-skill-files.md) study, not on a model. It failed safe - counted refused, nothing written - but the round trip the pair exists for was broken for every tick box. That the export prints `Yes` is itself owed a look on a real model. | **BUILT, NOT PROVEN.** IMPORT_PARAMETER_VALUES version 3 (DRAFT) takes a Yes/No the way WRITE_ELEMENT_PARAMETERS version 4 does ([5b-247](#)) and reads it back. | **316** | **A BLANK CELL IN A TEXT COLUMN WIPED THE VALUE ON THE ELEMENT, SILENTLY.** Found 2026-10-04 by reading the code, the same study. IMPORT_PARAMETER_VALUES version 2 handed an empty cell to a text parameter as an empty string, so an empty cell in a spreadsheet the modeller had trimmed cleared whatever the model held, and was counted as written. A number or integer blank was refused, so the same empty cell meant two different things by column. Whether an empty cell means *leave it* or *clear it* is the modeller's answer, and it was being assumed (D-33). | **BUILT, NOT PROVEN.** IMPORT_PARAMETER_VALUES version 3 (DRAFT) requires the answer for every run, with no default, and counts the cells it skipped. [Group CD](../needs-checking/group-cd.md). |
| **317** | **TWO FRAGMENTS LEFT REVIT'S SHARED PARAMETER FILE SETTING POINTED AT HERON'S PATH, WHERE NO UNDO REACHES.** Found 2026-10-04 by reading the code, the same study. ADD_PROJECT_PARAMETER and TRANSFER_PROJECT_PARAMETERS_BETWEEN_DOCUMENTS set the application's shared parameter file to the path they were given and never set it back. That setting belongs to Revit, not to the model: it is outside every transaction, so the one Undo that takes the parameter back leaves Manage > Shared Parameters pointing somewhere the modeller never chose (Article 8). ADD_FAMILY_SHARED_PARAMETERS reads the setting and never writes it. ADD_PROJECT_PARAMETER also bound categories without asking Revit whether each takes a bound parameter, and never compared what it asked for with what was bound, so a category Revit dropped was not named. | **BUILT, NOT PROVEN.** Both at version 2 (DRAFT): the previous setting is read first and put back whatever happens, and the reply says so; a category that cannot take the parameter, or that Revit dropped, is named. [Group CD](../needs-checking/group-cd.md). |
| **318** | **A PROFILE CORNER A HAIR ACROSS ITS AXIS PASSED THE REVOLVE'S OWN CHECK, AND REVIT BUILT A REVOLVE WITH NO SOLID IN IT.** Found 2026-10-04 on Family3 (unsaved family, Revit 2024, session 18156) building a power transformer. A porcelain bushing revolve on reference plane "Phase A" (facing left or right, X -1200), axisLine `-900.0,3100.0 -2775.0,6347.6` (tilted 30 degrees), built and read back. A turret revolve on the same plane and axis, profile `-900,3100; -696.5,3217.5; -1096.5,3910.3; -1035.9,3945.3; -1055.9,3979.9; -1320,3827.4` (radius 235 then 305, 840 mm along the axis), was refused by CREATE_FAMILY_REVOLUTION (FRG-GEO-039, PROVEN) with *"The revolve was built and has no extent to read back. NOTHING from this call was kept."* The same happened on Phase B and Phase C and with a variant shape; a ring at radius 232 to 240, touching no axis, built. **It looked like Revit merging a revolve that overlaps an earlier one. It is not.** Three rolled-back `validate --in "Family3" --write` runs, same session: (1) the turret as given - the same refusal; (2) the same turret moved 10 m up the plane, overlapping nothing - the same refusal; (3) the turret in its original place, still enclosing the bushing, with only the last corner moved 1 mm onto the profile's side, `-1319.13,3827.96` - **built**, X -1505.37 to -894.63, Y -1584.44 to -696.5, Z 2982.51 to 3980.12 mm, volume 150.458 L (pi x (0.305^2 x 0.04 + 0.235^2 x 0.8) m3 = 150.49 L), transaction group ROLLED BACK. The cause is the rounding: `-1320,3827.4` lies **0.034 mm on the far side** of the axis while every other corner is 235 or 305 mm on the near side. The fragment's side check ignores anything within `ShortCurveTolerance` (about 0.78 mm) of the axis, so the profile passed as touching it; Revit then built a revolve whose profile crosses its axis by a sliver, with no faces and no bounding box, and the fragment reported that as "no extent". Extrusions never showed it because they have no axis. | **FIXED - PROVEN again, signed by Ajmal PS on 2026-10-04 from the M_Fixed run below.** Before the curves are made, a corner lying within `ShortCurveTolerance` of the axis is put on the axis exactly, along its normal, and the reply says how many were moved; a side that this leaves shorter than Revit's shortest line is refused by name. The empty-solid refusal now names the likely cause. Run 2026-10-04 on a scratch copy of `M_Fixed` opened from Project2 (Revit 2024, session 18156; Family3 had been closed), every run rolled back: the turret exactly as given BUILT on "Center", volume 150.464 L, with "1 corner(s) lay within 0.78 mm of the axis and were put on it exactly"; the same turret with that corner 5 mm across, `-1324.33,3824.96`, refused as crossing its axis; the cylinder (21.208 L) and the 32 mm sphere (0.137 L) from the cases read as before, with no corner moved. Compiles on 2020 to 2027. The new proof replaces the 2026-10-03 one; like it, no second route by hand was run. |
| **319** | **A MODELLER ASKING HERON TO PLACE DOORS AND WINDOWS WAS HANDED THE LOADS TAKE-OFF, A READ.** Found 2026-10-04 by the session building a test building in Project2: `heron_lookup` *"place doors and windows hosted in walls at points"* answered REPORT_SPACE_ENVELOPE (*words #4 + nearness #3*), with SELECT_BY_HOST, CREATE_WALL, DIMENSION_WALL_OPENINGS and REPORT_DOOR_ROOM_LINKS behind it and PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY - the tool that places them - not in the five. The same session then found *"read the thermal U value of every floor type and roof type"* answered CREATE_ROOF, a MODIFY. **NOT A STALE INDEX:** the shared store's rows, searchable text and vectors equalled `main`'s cards for every card it held, and a private store built fresh from `main` gave the same answer, candidate for candidate. **TWO CARDS, MEASURED.** (1) The trained search model reads only the first 512 word-pieces of a card - identity, utterances, then the purpose (row [320](#)) - and #410 opened PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY's purpose with its version-3 sill rules. The card is far longer (measured 2026-10-04: 1,355 word-pieces), so the model stopped reading its door-and-wall text: the phrase's nearness rank for that card was **1** before #410 and **35** after, its cosine 0.46 then 0.22. (2) The words route matches a typed word as a **prefix**, so *walls* never matches *wall* and *doors* never *door*: every phrasing PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY declared was singular, while REPORT_SPACE_ENVELOPE's (#409) were plural and twice said *outside walls*. Before #409, and between #409 and #410, the door tool won the phrase. | **FIXED 2026-10-04 in the cards - routing, measured without Revit.** PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY: a plain summary of what a modeller asks it for now opens the purpose, the two rules that still hold sit above the sill rules and the history below them, five plural phrasings were added, and a comment says the top of the purpose is what the search reads. REPORT_SPACE_ENVELOPE: the one phrasing that named *outside walls*, where the take-off reads every face, now names *every space's walls, roofs, floors and glass with their U-values*, and one more asks *what U-value does each roof and floor type have* - roof and floor and not wall, because *wall* in it, measured that day, sent the outside-walls request straight back to the take-off. SELECT_BY_HOST: three question phrasings (row [321](#)) - one first worded *which doors and windows are in these walls* moved it one place nearer *isolate all the pipes* and tipped that request from ISOLATE_ELEMENTS to CAP_OPEN_PIPE_ENDS, a change, on a margin of 0.00004 that `main` already had; reworded, it does not. **Measured on private stores built from the branch, on the trained model the owner's PC uses:** the four phrasings of the brief 0 then 4 of 4; 34 door-placement phrasings 1 then 30, 26 of them written before any wording was chosen; the take-off's own 15 phrasings 15 then 15; twelve neighbouring requests unchanged; the owner's 79 questions (`tools/score-routing.py`) unchanged but for #62, *not found* then 3rd; the session's U-value question CREATE_ROOF then REPORT_SPACE_ENVELOPE, a READ - though it reads the U-value only of the types around Spaces - and of six U-value phrasings written before any wording, 3 went to a change on `main` and none after. **On spelling alone, as CI ranks:** the brief 2 then 3 of 4 (*add windows to the outside walls* goes to DIMENSION_WALL_OPENINGS), placement 16 then 24 of 34, the owner's 79 unchanged, the session's U-value question CREATE_ROOF then REPORT_SPACE_ENVELOPE, and 3 then 2 of the six on a change. Guarded by [`tests/test_routing_phrases.py`](../../tests/test_routing_phrases.py), on the trained model, watched failing 5 checks on `main`'s cards, and by the U-value question added to [`check-risk-crossings`](../../tools/check-risk-crossings.py). **Live on the owner's PC** once the merge is in the main checkout, the shared store rebuilt from it and the Claude app restarted. |
| **320** | **THE TRAINED SEARCH MODEL READS ONLY THE FIRST 512 WORD-PIECES OF A CARD, AND A LONG CARD'S OPENING DECIDES WHAT IT IS NEAR.** Found 2026-10-04 beside row [319](#). `heron_embed._text_for` joins identity, capability, domain, utterances and the whole purpose, and `model2vec`'s `encode` cuts at `max_length=512` unless told otherwise - nothing in Heron said so. Measured 2026-10-04: 126 of the 469 cards are longer than that. So *whatever opens a long card's purpose is what nearness thinks the card is*: a new version's notes written at the top, as #410 did, silently move a tool away from its own job, and history paragraphs pull questions toward the tool they describe. On PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY as it stood on `main`, the same text against row 319's phrase: cosine 0.50 cut at 128, 0.39 at 256, 0.22 at 512, 0.32 uncut. Every routing check read past it: `check-routing` asks declared words, which answer by identity, and CI ranks by spelling, which has no cut. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** Changing what is embedded - identity, utterances and the purpose's first paragraph, say - re-ranks every card on the owner's PC, and needs its own change measured against his 79 questions. Until then the rule is in PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY's card as a comment: **a purpose opens with what a modeller asks the tool for, and a new version's rules go below that, never above it.** A check naming the cards whose first 512 word-pieces never reach their own job would find the next one before a modeller does. |

Row 319 gave SELECT_BY_HOST three question phrasings and went no further. The repair is SELECT_BY_HOST's own card - doors and windows first, a count beside the selection - and *what is the sill height of these windows* sent to READ_ELEMENT_PARAMETERS: each its own change, measured on the same 22 questions. |

| **330** | **THE LOADS TAKE-OFF COUNTS A CURTAIN PANEL WHOLE ON A SPACE'S FACE, AND REFUSES THE SPACE WHEN THE PANELS EXCEED IT.** Found 2026-10-06 in Heron loads test, Group CC rows CC8 and CC22. Each Space did get only its own panels; it is their area that is wrong. A window or door is still counted whole: its place is its box's middle, which a frame or sill moves. **Found fixing it, and fixed with it:** the frames rule of the second review (N5) waited for a `curtain` key the fragment never writes, so a curtain wall with mullions would have refused its Space for the curtain wall type's missing U; `curtain()` now knows one by its panels. Run on the take-off kept from this test (`a76b5e966e1e3ab6`, read-only copy): Office 103 and Meeting Room 105 calculate - every curtain face nets to glass, 22.416 m2 on 22.416, 18.641 on 18.641, 17.947 on 17.949 - Offices 101 and 102 are unchanged at 4137.6 and 2176.7 W, and Office 201 is refused only for its real missing value, the skylight type's U (CC20). | **331** | **THE LOADS TAKE REVIT'S LAYER-ONLY U AS AN AIR-TO-AIR U, AND ROUND PEOPLE TO WHOLE PERSONS.** Found 2026-10-06 by the hand check of Office 101 ([Group CC](../needs-checking/group-cc.md)). Revit fills a Basic Wall's, Floor's or Roof's U as 1 / (the sum of its LAYERS' resistances), with no inside or outside surface resistance - Generic - 225mm Masonry reads R 0.1731 = 0.225 m / 1.30 W/(m.K) - and the engine uses it as the whole assembly's U, where ASHRAE's U-factors include the films (Fundamentals Ch. 26). On a well-insulated wall that is 4 % (0.2306 against 0.2222); on the 100 mm blockwork partition to the unconditioned store it is 8.78 against 2.83, and Office 101's total is 4.138 kW against 3.173 kW by hand - 30 % high. Separately, 27.035 m2 x 0.05 people/m2 = 1.35 people became 1, in the people gain and in the outdoor air's Pz. Both move design figures, so how they are handled is the owner's to decide (D-33). | **OPEN - recorded for the owner, not fixed.** With Heron's own assumptions the hand check gives 4.153 kW, 0.4 % from Heron's 4.138: the arithmetic and the sun are right. |

| **345** | **`heron_takeoff.azimuth_deg` TURNS EVERY FACE THE WRONG WAY ON A PROJECT WHOSE TRUE NORTH IS ROTATED.** Measured 2026-10-06 while proving SET_PROJECT_LOCATION on scratch Project2 (Revit 2024, session 51820). Revit stores True North as `ProjectPosition.Angle`, and REPORT_SPACE_ENVELOPE hands that angle on as `project_to_true_north_deg`. An angle of +30 degrees is what Revit's own Manage > Location, Site tab shows as *Angle from Project North to True North: 30.00 deg East* - the owner read the dialog himself - and Revit's shared position of two points along project north (Report Shared Coordinates) puts project north at compass bearing **330** for it, and at 12.5 for a stored angle of -12.5 (*12.5 West*). `azimuth_deg` ADDS the angle: a wall facing project north comes out at **30**, where the true bearing is 330, so every facade's sun is mirrored about north whenever True North is turned. Its own docstring names this place as where the sign is fixed, and [`tests/test_takeoff.py`](../../tests/test_takeoff.py)'s *"turned 30 degrees east of true north: a face to project north faces 30"* asserts the assumption, not a measurement. The C# reads the angle correctly. NEEDS-CHECKING CC4 asked exactly this question. | **FIXED 2026-10-07 in the brain (PR #433), NOT YET RUN on a take-off.** `azimuth_deg` takes the angle OFF the project bearing - (project bearing - angle) - and its docstring names this row and the measurement. [`tests/test_takeoff.py`](../../tests/test_takeoff.py) now holds a face to project north at **330** for a stored +30 and at **12.5** for -12.5, and a face to project east at 60 for +30, so a north mirrored rather than turned fails too; [`tests/test_loads_view.py`](../../tests/test_loads_view.py) turns a face to project south to true east for a stored +90 (it asserted west), and a new check holds the 3D view's compass to the Site tab - before the fix it leaned 30 degrees WEST for *30 East*. Each new check was seen to fail on the old sign first. **Nothing else needed changing**: the take-off sheet's facing column, the glass-by-facing totals, the facing colours and the compass (through `north_xy`) all take their bearing from this one function, and the C# is unchanged. The run of 2026-10-06 on Heron loads test had True North at 0 (CC17), where the two signs agree, so none of its results moves. The take-off run on a turned project is [Group CC](../needs-checking/group-cc.md) CC4, owed: on 2026-10-07 the open Revit 2024 held Project1, with no MEP Spaces, and the turn needs the owner's Admin switch ([Group CJ](../needs-checking/group-cj.md) CJ10). |

| **396** | **QUESTIONS ABOUT WHAT AN ELEMENT IS MADE OF REACH WRITES - 29 OF 69 ON THE LEXICAL BACKEND, 20 ON THE TRAINED ONE - AND EIGHT CARD-ONLY FIXES EACH BROKE SOMETHING ELSE.** Measured 2026-10-09 on main `17040842` (480 fragments, no Revit filter) through `heron_brain.lookup` on scratch stores, on the lexical backend and the trained one (`model2vec` potion-base-8M, in a venv outside the repository), against [`tests/data/material-questions.tsv`](../../tests/data/material-questions.tsv): 238 sentences no card declares - 44 written before any fix was tried, the rest by skeptics set to break the candidate fixes. Of its 69 questions, 29 reach a write on lexical (`CREATE_MATERIAL` 15, `REPLACE_MATERIAL` 5) and 20 on trained (`CREATE_MATERIAL` 12, `REPLACE_MATERIAL` 2). The core shapes are *"what/which material(s) does/do these, those, it, the walls use"* and *"what material is assigned to those beams"*; row [375](#)'s two were the first seen. **WHY, measured:** (1) the question's words come from INCIDENTAL text on the writes - `CREATE_MATERIAL`'s purpose says *in use*, *those*, *made*; `SET_MATERIAL_COLOUR`'s *those are different facts*; `REPLACE_MATERIAL`'s identity *these elements use it* - while `READ_ELEMENT_MATERIAL` says neither *use* nor *those*, and every other word in these questions (*what, which, does, this, are, that*) is on more than half the cards and scores nothing. (2) A query word of three letters or fewer is matched exactly (`heron_search._fts_query`), so *use* never reaches *using*, *unused* or *used* - which is all `FIND_UNUSED_MATERIALS`, the card for the negated questions, says. (3) On the trained backend a swap between the two routes fuses to an exact tie (row [236](#)), and these cards sit on many ties decided by under one rank. **EIGHT CARD-ONLY FIXES, NONE KEPT.** Each was measured on both backends, with and without PR #450's `LIST_MATERIALS`, and the qualifying ones were given to skeptics writing fresh sentences. *use* or *those* on `READ_ELEMENT_MATERIAL` took *"which materials do we never use"* from `FIND_UNUSED_MATERIALS` and *"create a material those ducts can use"* from `CREATE_MATERIAL`. Rewording `CREATE_MATERIAL`'s purpose alone took the first 19 questions from 5 to 3 and 2 to 1 and no further; one rewording brought in *created* and *replaced* and sent *"what material was this duct created with"* to the write. Material names on `REPLACE_MATERIAL` sent *"find the elements made of stainless steel"* to it. Purpose hygiene on the three writes moved almost nothing (5 to 4). The best balance across four cards halved the skeptics' crossings (24 to 12, 18 to 7) but its author's own fresh sentences found a read sent to a write. *finish*, *assigned* and *got* on the read took *"which materials has nothing been assigned"* off `FIND_UNUSED_MATERIALS` and sent *"what comments has this wall got"* to `CREATE_WALL`. Rewording the two reads' purposes sent *"which materials can go"* to `TRANSFER_MATERIALS_BETWEEN_DOCUMENTS`. | **OPEN - recorded; no card was changed.** Every card-only fix measured trades crossings here for regressions elsewhere, because these cards sit on ties: the fix belongs in retrieval, not in a card. The two places the measurements point at are the exact match for short words (*use* against *using* and *unused*) and the equal-weight tie between the routes, which row 236 left alone because changing it cost the owner's questions. Any such change is measured on the owner's 79 questions first ([D-100](../DECISIONS.md)) and on this file second, on both backends. Until then `heron_lookup` still warns when a question is answered by a write with a read close behind it, and a write still needs the owner's Changes switch. Main moved under the measurement (PR #460 changed `REPLACE_MATERIAL` and `SELECT_BY_MATERIAL`): the counts are the same on `e21a3f5e` and `17040842`, and one request, *"create a copper material for the pipes"*, moved from one wrong card to another on the trained backend. |
